# Decisions log

Behaviour the maintainers decided on purpose. A change to a row below needs a
new decision that names it (maintainer, date, issue); an agent or contributor
PR cannot make it silently. Each row names the test that pins the behaviour; a
row decided before its code says "not yet pinned" and names the issue whose
test will pin it.

Two golden fixtures pin the whole command surface and fail on any added,
removed or changed row: `crates/signex-app/src/menu_bar/testdata/menu_bar.golden.tsv`
(menus) and `crates/signex-app/src/keymap/testdata/{catalog,builtin_profiles}.golden.tsv`
(catalog and built-in shortcuts, regenerate with `UPDATE_GOLDEN=1`).

| Date | Decision | Decided by | Issue | Pinned by |

| 2026-11-02 | An `AppCommandId` behaves the same on every surface (context menu, palette, keymap, menus), so a CLI or an agent can run the same id with the same effect. Surface context travels as `CommandArgs` (the invocation context a GUI surface fills: click point, project; operands are filled only by a CLI and no GUI caller sets one), never as a surface-only message. Every context-menu command row dispatches its id. Visible changes approved with it: (a) the Cut and Copy rows are greyed with nothing selected, so Copy no longer overwrites the system clipboard with an empty string; (b) the project tree's Save row uses the `save_document` gate (greyed when Save would write nothing, live in symbol and footprint tabs); (c) the canvas Project Options row and the tree Save row close their menu after the pick; (d) Place ▸ Net Label, the palette and the keys arm the NET ghost and remember the Active Bar group, and picking any tool ends a pending port session (#1920). | Hakan (approved (a)-(d) 2026-11-03; Caner reviewed #1935, no blockers) | #1727, #1920 | `catalog_agreement_tests::command_rows_read_label_and_shortcut_from_the_catalog`, `context_menu::tests::row_effects`, `command::args::tests`, `dispatch::tool::tests::a_label_tool_pick_after_a_port_places_a_net_label`, `dispatch::tool::tests::selecting_a_tool_drops_a_pending_port`, `command::bridge::tests::the_newly_wired_ids_reach_their_named_messages` |
| 2026-11-03 | Extends the one-id-one-behaviour decision to the main menu bar: a row that carries a command id (the in-window bar, an undocked window's bar and the macOS menu) runs that id through `dispatch_command`, and `dispatch_command_reporting` says whether a command ran, was unknown, had no bridge arm, or was disabled and why (a menu click that cannot run is logged). Two rows first kept their own action because their id did something else (#1991); both now run an id: View ▸ Cycle Grid Size runs `next_grid`, Design ▸ Electrical Rules Check runs the new `open_erc_dialog` (Caner 2026-11-03). Behaviour changes: (1) View ▸ Toggle Grid saves the choice like the keys and the status bar; (2) in an undocked window's bar, command rows run against that window, so View ▸ Fit All fits that window's canvas; (3) Place ▸ Wire, Bus and Junction run the Active Bar action: they remember the tool as the wiring default and close an open Active Bar dropdown; (4) the alias pairs `place_wire`/`draw_wire`, `place_bus`/`draw_bus`, `place_text`/`place_text_string`, `draw_graphic_line`/`draw_line`, `draw_hierarchical_sheet`/`place_sheet_symbol` share the Active Bar route, and `draw_wire` and `draw_bus` need a schematic like `place_wire` and `place_bus`; (5) View ▸ Fit All reaches the PCB renderer as a camera move (no geometry dirtied); (6) View ▸ Zoom In / Zoom Out are greyed without a schematic or PCB (the key is dropped there too); (7) Design ▸ Annotation ▸ Annotate Quietly / Force Annotate are greyed with no sheet open (the key still logs that there is nothing to do; the palette greys it); (8) Tools ▸ Document Options is greyed unless the active tab is a symbol or footprint editor inside an open library; (9) the first-run tour card is shown once (the pref is saved when it opens) and closes only on ✕, Esc or a pointer gesture on a canvas, not on a hover or a Home / menu Fit All (#1994). (10) The Save disabled reason now reads "Save needs a schematic or editor document, or a project with unsaved changes" (it can reach the Messages panel through a refused window command). Changes (1) to (10) and the route choice are approved by Hakan 2026-11-03; Caner review pending on #1997. | Hakan (approved (1)-(10) 2026-11-03) | #1922, #1928, #1994, #1991 | `menu_bar::tests::walk`, `menu_bar::golden_tests::model_shortcuts_follow_the_active_keymap`, `dead_schematic_controls` (regression), `menu_bar::tests::{view_rows,window_native_rows,help_tools_rows,place_design_rows}`, `app::command::tests`, `app::command::enablement::tests`, `app::dispatch::first_run_tour_tests`, `canvas::view_gesture_tests`, `menu_bar.golden.tsv` |

| 2026-11-04 | Schematic sheet text scales with the drawing at every zoom: no minimum and no maximum on-screen size from signex (was 6 to 64 px). Text under 0.5 device px is not drawn, and a size that is not a number, or past the sanity ceiling, is skipped as hostile; the ceiling is derived (100 mm text, over the smallest cap-height ratio `mm_per_em` can return, at the zoom limit on a 4x display, doubled), so real text never reaches it. Past iced's own atlas limit (`MAX_ATLAS_TEXT_SIZE`, 1024 logical px, scaled by the display for signex's GPU staging) signex's atlas pipeline leaves the text to the canvas, where alplabai/iced draws it as vector outlines at true size (see the next rows). The GPU glyph pipeline no longer wraps sheet text at the viewport width (deep-zoom notes would wrap on the GPU only). A sheet loaded with a text size above 100 mm keeps 100 mm, a size that is not a number reads as the default, and the repair is reported in Messages. The Component Preview, the PCB surface and the symbol editor keep their own clamps. | Hakan | #2022 | `scene_shader::tests::sheet_text_is_its_true_size_at_every_zoom_on_both_paths`, `text::size_tests::text_past_the_atlas_ceiling_is_drawn_at_true_size_not_clamped_or_culled`, `text::size_tests::a_hostile_file_of_huge_texts_is_skipped_at_max_zoom`, `oversize_text_tests::oversize_starts_just_past_the_iced_atlas_limit_at_every_display_scale`, `text_size_repair::tests::parsing_a_file_with_an_absurd_text_size_clamps_it`, `symbol_canvas::tests::the_preview_keeps_its_text_clamp` |
| 2026-11-04 | Generic text and vector rendering lives in the alplabai/iced fork, not in signex: canvas text past the glyph-atlas limit is drawn by iced as Lyon-filled glyph outlines (NonZero), so it stays at true size at any zoom. Signex carries only schematic policy: the true-size scale, the hostile-size load clamp and its Messages log, the per-snapshot text-width cache, viewport culling before text reaches iced, and which surface draws what. Vello stays a long-term evaluation (alplabai/iced#21). Signex pins the fork at `iced-signex-v0.15.0-alpha.4` (rev `a94392b0b`, fork PR alplabai/iced#23 plus the alpha.4 fixes). Visible effects of alpha.4: the Active Bar's power and net-label dropdown icons draw the `<text>` labels their SVG files carry, and the monitor size is read as logical on scaled displays. | Hakan | #2022 | `renderer_scene_canvas::line_geometry_tests::oversize_text_keeps_its_true_size_and_goes_to_the_canvas_beside_the_gpu`, fork `iced_graphics::geometry::text::tests` |
| 2026-11-04 | Oversize text handed to iced is bounded: at most 64 oversize texts per frame, each cut to 256 characters, with one Messages warning per run when a bound is hit. Off-screen oversize text (padded by 1 em) never reaches iced. Caching the tessellated outlines belongs in the fork (alplabai/iced#21). | Hakan | #2022 | `oversize_text_tests::a_flood_of_long_oversize_texts_is_bounded_and_warns_once` |
| 2026-11-04 | What the canvas shows is what is written: the persist, close and export handlers themselves commit pending title-block drafts first, one undo step per field. That covers File menu Save, Ctrl+S and the palette, undocked windows, Save As, Save All, PDF, netlist, BOM and ERC report exports, print, Quit, the project tree's and File's Close Project (that project's drafts only; its drafts are committed before the unsaved-changes gate, so a draft-only sheet gets the prompt), Remove (its unsaved-edits prompt counts a draft-only sheet; Cancel leaves the draft a draft, Save commits then saves it, Discard drops it), and the user's own tab close (that sheet's drafts only, so it parks dirty like any edit). Discard in the Remove prompt drops the drafts under the target and never writes them, and closing an unrelated tab touches no other sheet's draft. A closed sheet's drafts are pruned with its engine. There is exactly one draft per (sheet, field) or (sheet, paper edge), in one store on `DocumentState` (`panels::PanelDrafts`), whichever window typed it; panel contexts hold a handle to it only so the Properties view can read it, so a window context cloned from the main one, or swapped out for a window action, can neither hide a draft nor bring a stale one back, and undocking or reattaching moves nothing. Every action and the sheet-frame preview in any window read that store. A commit is scoped to what the action touches: Save and Save As commit that document's sheets, Save All and Quit everything, Close Project that project's, and an export, print, BOM, ERC or netlist the document's project (the print preview's own Export commits nothing, its snapshot is already taken); another project's drafts stay pending and its sheets clean. Renaming a sheet moves its pending drafts, title-block and paper-edge, to the new path. A draft on another open sheet goes through the read-only guard (a newer-minor sheet refuses it and says so in Messages) and marks that sheet modified. While typing, a field previews on the sheet without touching the document; Enter commits it. | Hakan | #2027 | `title_block_persist_tests::*` (menu row, keymap command, quit prompt, close project, tree Close Project prompt, Discard, unrelated tab, own tab close, read-only sheet, undocked-window drafts at Quit/Save/rename/Close Project, stale-clone and window-Save regressions, per-project and per-document scoping), `title_block_fields::drafts_before_persist::*`, `title_block_fields::a_typed_draft_previews_on_the_canvas_before_it_commits` |
| 2026-11-04 | A text's box (selection, hit test, box select, autoplace-adjacent geometry) is as wide as the shaped text in the family it draws in: its own font, else the sheet default font, an uninstalled font resolving to the bundled face that draws instead. It is re-measured when the default font changes, on undo and on redo. Global and hierarchical label flag bodies and their SVG/PDF text offset follow the measured width (the flag around Iosevka text is about 14% shorter than the old 0.58 em per character). | Hakan | #2023 | `font_geometry_tests::{default_face_box_width_is_the_shaped_advance, a_non_default_family_gets_its_own_width, a_missing_family_is_boxed_in_the_face_that_draws, the_document_default_font_remeasures_unfonted_items_through_undo_and_redo, iosevka_geometry_is_the_shaped_advance}`, `svg::labels::tests` |

Explicit widths and the floor are unchanged. | Hakan | #2025 | `defaults_tests::{a_zero_symbol_stroke_is_the_default_wire_width, a_symbol_strokes_at_its_most_common_library_width}`, `symbol_geometry_tests::a_width_zero_symbol_strokes_its_pins_at_the_default_wire_width` |
| 2026-11-05 | A power port draws its body strokes, pin and Value text in the theme's power colour on the canvas and in SVG / PDF. One width rule covers every stored stroke (symbol graphics, pins, drawings, child sheet frames, wires) and their previews and ghosts: a stored 0 draws at 0.254 mm, an explicit width is floored at 0.15 mm; no-connect 0.2 and label marker 0.15 stay fixed; bus 0.45 is the built-in bus default, which a project may change (#2026). Power ports: 2.54 mm stem, 1.27 mm for the hanging grounds (power ground, signal ground, earth), stock library ports (GND, VCC, +3V3, +5V) regenerated to match (Caner, 2026-11-05; the stem lengths are superseded by the 2026-11-21 power-port stem row below: every style uses the 1.27 mm stem). | Caner | #2091 | `symbol_tests::{a_power_port_draws_in_the_power_colour_and_a_part_keeps_its_own, a_power_port_strokes_at_the_stock_symbol_width, a_child_sheet_frame_resolves_its_stored_width_through_the_one_rule}`, `drawings_tests::a_width_zero_drawing_draws_at_the_default_stroke`, `defaults_tests::every_stored_stroke_kind_resolves_zero_to_the_default`, `svg::stroke_rule_tests` |
| 2026-11-05 | The stock resistor (R) is a 5.08 x 1.524 mm rectangle body and the stock inductor (L) has 0.635 mm humps, both with 1.27 mm leads and pins at ±3.81 mm, the same length as the other passives; the stock ground power port has a 1.27 mm stem. Pin tips do not move, so saved wires stay connected; sheets that embedded a stock R or L show the library-update notice (Caner, 2026-11-05). | Caner | #2101 | `stock_r_and_l_keep_their_pin_tips`, `every_stock_symbol_pin_is_on_the_schematic_grid_and_converts`, `every_style_has_the_one_step_stem_and_the_tip_stays_on_the_origin` |
| 2026-11-04 | Anti-aliasing (MSAA 4x) is a preference, default on, applies at startup; the grid snaps to device pixels (Hakan, 2026-11-04). Hairlines and wires already snapped (#1887, #1902); grid lines and dots now use the same snap, so they stay crisp with multisampling. A saved prefs.json without the key reads as on. The fork's wgpu passes `MSAAx4` straight to texture creation with no capability check and no fallback: WebGPU guarantees 4x for every renderable surface format, so an adapter is not expected to reject it, and if one ever did the escape hatch is the preference (off), not code. | Hakan | #2022 | `fonts::tests::{an_absent_antialiasing_key_defaults_to_on, a_saved_antialiasing_value_round_trips}`, `grid::snap_tests::{grid_lines_are_whole_device_pixels_at_one_and_two_times, grid_dots_sit_on_device_pixel_middles}` |
| 2026-11-05 | Schematic primitives take their defaults from one table (`SchematicDefaults`); a project may override any of them in `.snxprj` `schematic_defaults`. Built-in values are what sheets draw today: wire 0.254 mm, bus 0.45 mm, symbol line and pin = wire, shape 0.254 mm (the one stored-stroke rule of the power-port row above: a stored 0 draws at 0.254 mm, an explicit width is floored at 0.15 mm; a project override replaces the 0.254; behaviour change: a line, rectangle, circle or arc placed with Width untouched now stores the shape default, 0.254 mm built in (trunk stored 0.15), or the project's override), junction 0.7 mm (radius floor 0.35 mm), text 10 pt Iosevka, wire line style Solid, new-shape fill none. Precedence is explicit item value > net class > project > built-in, so (behaviour change) a wire's own width now beats its class width; a bus has no width or class of its own. Bus entries follow the project wire default and child-sheet frames the project shape stroke default. Wire and bus hit tolerance and their selection boxes use the drawn (project) widths, and the power-port placement ghost strokes with the project defaults. Symbol boxes and the PDF fit-to-page box use the symbol geometry and stored widths, not the resolved stroke, as on trunk. Line style applies to wires whose class sets none (a wire stores none); shape fill and font apply to placement only (a placed label or note stores the project font), so no stored value is reinterpreted. The single allowed visual change: exported junctions use the canvas size and floor instead of a 0.7 mm literal. | Hakan | #2026 | `schematic::defaults::tests::*`, `project::schematic_defaults_tests::*`, `schematic_runtime::defaults_tests::*`, `wire_style_render_tests::a_project_wire_width_widens_the_hit_area_and_the_box`, `project_tab::schematic_defaults_tests::*` |

Measured on a 2400-symbol sheet, neither meets 8 ms per frame while rotated text is drawn per frame (about 0.14 ms per text, canvas and GPU fallback alike). | 2026-11-06 | A pin has one electrical type, `signex_types::schematic::PinDirection` (14 variants), shared by the symbol editor, `.snxsym` and the schematic; the library's own 10-variant enum is gone. A `.snxsym` writes the type through one token table (`PinDirection::as_token`). The ten tokens the old enum wrote keep their text and their meaning: `Power` is `PowerInput`, `OpenCollector` is `OpenDrainLow`, `OpenEmitter` is `OpenDrainHigh`, `NotConnected` is `DoNotConnect`, `Tristate` is `ThreeStatable`, `Unspecified` is `Unclassified`, so a file that uses only those types re-saves byte-identical and an older build still reads it. The four newer types write `PowerOutput`, `GroundReference`, `Differential`, `Clock`; an older build refuses a symbol file holding one of them (unknown token) instead of guessing. The symbol-editor type dropdown offers all 14. | Caner | #2108 | `pin_token::tests::*`, `symbol::tests::{legacy_pin_tokens_write_back_byte_identical, every_pin_type_survives_a_snxsym_round_trip, stock_snxsym_re_encodes_byte_identical}`, `to_lib_symbol_tests::{pin_direction_converts_losslessly_for_every_variant, legacy_pin_tokens_convert_to_their_old_schematic_type}` |

| 2026-11-08 | Maintainer decisions of 2026-11-07 and 2026-11-08. (1) A power port's net-name text is fixed to its port and cannot be moved on its own. | 2026-11-06 | The command catalog gains an `operands` column (#1934): the operands a CLI or agent can pass instead of a pointer click or a dialog answer, as `name:kind[/unit]`, `?` when optional (`at:point/mm`, `rotation:angle/deg?`, `path:path`, `overwrite:bool?`). One naming convention: snake_case names, points `x,y` in mm, angles in degrees, GUI answers `path` / `overwrite`. `gui_only` now means the command has no operand form. 20 rows get operands: the placement commands with a plain-value method (`place_net_label`, `place_local_net_label`, `place_global_label`, `place_hierarchical_label`, `place_power_symbol`, `place_junction`, `place_no_connect`, `place_no_erc`, `place_wire`, `draw_wire`, `place_bus`, `draw_bus`, `place_text`, `place_text_string`, `draw_line`, `draw_graphic_line`, `draw_rectangle`) and the file-dialog commands (`open_document`, `new_document`, `save_document_as`). The operands are the inputs a command needs; everything else (line width, fill, wire mode, label shape, font and justification) comes from the pre-placement form and the project defaults, as for a click. A text operand is non-empty and has no control characters, a rotation is a multiple of 90 within ±360, stored as 0, 90, 180 or 270 (the form's choices), a path is absolute on the host. The 17 placement rows were `gui_only` and no longer are (141 to 124 `gui_only` rows); nothing else changes, and a placement id run without operands still only arms its tool. The 17 placement commands run their operands (#2155) through their operand handlers (`command::placement_operands`): the edit goes through the same `place_*_at` commit a click ends in (undo, dirty and the Messages log as for a click), with none of the click-only follow-up (no tool arming or reset, repeat-placement memory, wire continuation or shape anchor). A point is used as given in mm; one a click would snap elsewhere (onto the grid, and for a wire also, with the electrical grid (Shift+E) on, onto a pin end, wire end or junction within the capture radius) is refused (`Disabled`, naming the grid and where a click lands) rather than moved, and a zero-length wire, bus, line or rectangle is refused. On a read-only sheet (#1402) the run is refused with the read-only reason. The file-dialog commands have no handler yet, so their operands are refused (`Disabled`, "not run yet") until the second #2155 PR. | Hakan | #1934, #2155 | `catalog.golden.tsv`, `command::operands::tests::every_descriptor_follows_the_one_convention`, `command::operands::tests`, `command::placement_operand_tests`, `command::operand_handler_tests::every_operand_command_has_a_handler_or_is_refused` |
| 2026-11-07 | Internationalisation stack: Project Fluent (`.ftl`, `fluent-bundle`) for messages, restricted to the MF2-convertible subset (variables, plural and string selectors, terms; no custom functions, no message references inside selectors); individual ICU4X crates (`icu_decimal`, `icu_datetime` + `icu_time`, `icu_list`, `icu_casemap`, `icu_locale_core`), never the `icu` meta-crate, pinned to the 2.2 line already in `Cargo.lock` (via `icu_locale_core`); compiled data with dead-code elimination, `icu4x-datagen` + `ICU4X_DATA_DIR` only if a measured binary growth needs it, no `build.rs` data generation. Language negotiation is RFC 4647 lookup through `fluent-langneg` (`tr-TR` → `tr` → `en-US`). | Hakan | #2053, #2054 | not yet pinned; `signex-i18n` crate tests (#2055) |

| 2026-11-08 | A sheet embeds one library-symbol body per version, keyed by a body ref `<lib_id>#<8 hex FNV-1a of the canonical body>` (`Symbol.body_ref`; `Symbol.lib_id` stays the library identity). A new placement always draws with the library's current symbol, whatever the sheet already holds; parts already placed keep the version they were placed with until Properties > Update from library, which moves every instance of the part on the sheet (default) or only the chosen part ("Only this one"); all units of a part always move together, and a refused update (connected pins or units would be lost) changes nothing. Old and new versions coexist on one sheet. A version nothing draws with after an update is dropped from the sheet; undo restores it. Two different bodies under one ref are an error, never a merge. A sheet saved before this change is upgraded once on load (the single removable exception to the no-legacy rule); the next save writes the new form. This replaces the earlier rule that a sheet's first copy of a symbol wins. Decided by Caner: the user always gets the current library body; old versions coexist; the sheet auto-upgrades on load; Update applies to all instances on the sheet, with "Only this one". The writer is `snxsch/1.1`, so builds from #977 on open these files read-only; the paste fragment marker is `signex-clipboard-fragment/2`. | Caner | #2209 | `regression::library_place_symbol`, `signex_engine::exec::replace_lib_symbol_tests`, `signex_types::format::tests` (versions, upgrade, collision) |

| 2026-11-09 (name rule frozen 2026-11-10, 6761836527) | `place_wire`, `draw_wire`, `place_bus` and `draw_bus` from operands are checked before they commit (#2199); for these four this supersedes the 2026-11-06 row's "same `place_*_at` commit": they commit through the click's `apply_engine_commands`, but only after the check, and they answer with data. A wire is checked for its net diff and its geometry; a bus for its geometry (orthogonal, body, dangling end) and, with the one bus analysis before and after, for what it pulls in (another run, an entry, a bus-name label: refused, no escape) and for joining buses whose names the name rule below calls a short. Merging on purpose happens (a second name for a signal, tying AGND to GND at one point), so the caller states it with `allow_merge=true` and an agent never does it silently. A point operand also takes a pin reference `<designator>.<pin>` (split at the first `.`, the pin number first, then a unique pin name), resolved on the sheet the edit targets and accepted off the grid. A wire from operands is refused when its path also touches another pin, label, junction or wire, when it would join two differently named nets (`allow_merge=true` allows it), when it crosses a symbol body, is not horizontal and vertical, or leaves an end connected to nothing (`allow_open=true` allows it); `dry_run=true` reports without changing anything; an accepted run answers with its segments, net and merged names. A short or rename is decided on the project's nets by identity, not on name text (net scope: `docs/NET_NAMING.md`), by the name rule frozen in https://github.com/alplabai/signex/issues/2199#issuecomment-6761836527, which supersedes the name-rule parts of all earlier #2199 comments: (1) a net's own names are its net labels, global labels/ports and power ports (power flags name nothing), its aliases the names of hierarchical labels; (2) two own-named nets with different own-name sets are a short; (3) an own-named net joined to an alias-only net joins freely and the answer lists the aliases; (4) two alias-only nets that share no name are a short, sharing one they join freely (`{SDA, I2C_DATA}` + `SDA`); (5) a label or power port adding an own name the net does not carry (own or alias) to a net with own names is a rename, and a hierarchical label never renames; (6) each edit is judged by what it changes: a child's local names already behind a hierarchical label before the edit play no part on the parent, seen from the edited sheet (on the edited sheet and its ancestors they stay names; only names below or beside it are hidden), names the edit itself brings behind a port count, so the same final design reached by another edit order can get another verdict; (7) buses follow the same rule with bus names (hierarchical bus labels are aliases, and a bus-name label at a run end that sits on no run takes part) and a bus run ending on a sheet entry is connected. Limit: bus names are judged on the edited sheet only; the project check compares member nets, not bus names, so a bus name reaching the run from another sheet is not seen. One function (`signex_net::join_rule`) holds the rule for the per-sheet, bus and project checks; findings only a design-level rule could settle are ERC's. A bus run reaches other sheets when its run (by the bus analysis) has a hierarchical or global bus-name label or a sheet pin; a no-connect and a power flag are not judged. A net that reaches no sheet pin, hierarchical or global label or power port is judged on its sheet only. When the project cannot be read completely (a sheet missing or unreadable, or a child reference leaving its folder) and the net reaches another sheet, the edit is refused with the reason unless `allow_merge=true` (Hakan, https://github.com/alplabai/signex/issues/2199#issuecomment-6749073920). The check reads the project's hierarchy itself, so it never waits for the app's walk. Where the project check runs (the sheet is in a project and the net or bus run reaches another sheet), its net-name verdict is the only one; the per-sheet name check decides loose sheets and the rest. A point operand also takes `<sheet name>.<entry name>`, the connection point of that sheet pin of a sheet symbol on the sheet, accepted off the grid, refused for no such sheet or entry, two sheet symbols or two entries of that name, or a name a part and a sheet both carry; its end names exactly that sheet pin (Hakan, https://github.com/alplabai/signex/issues/2199#issuecomment-6749265583). A bus takes no such reference, as it takes no pin reference (Hakan, https://github.com/alplabai/signex/issues/2199#issuecomment-6746103120). The point placements (the labels, `place_power_symbol`, `place_junction`, `place_no_connect`) are checked by one rule, as a wire is: the real command is applied to a copy and the copy is diffed with the netlist and the bus analysis. A short or rename by the name rule is refused unless `allow_merge=true`; by pin reference, anything not on the pin's net that would end up on it is refused with no escape. An answer of a wire or bus run carries `ends` (the reference and the resolved point of each end). The 17 placement commands take an optional `document` (an id or a path): given, the command runs on that sheet, never on the active tab, and the placements stay off the agent channel (their `document` is optional, the allowlist wants a required one); an attached CLI run refuses a bare placement and says it reaches a running Signex only through `apply_edits`. Rules: `docs/CLI.md`, "Wire intent", "Why allow_merge", "Labels, power ports, junctions and no-connects" and "Bus geometry". | Maintainer (issue #2199, 2026-11-08) and Hakan (#2199 comments, 2026-11-09: pin-ref syntax, off-grid tips, bus geometry with wires, labels, power ports and junctions refused when they would rename or short a net unless allow_merge, bus runs intent-checked too, pin-ref join checks; the optional `document` operand: Hakan, #2199 comment 2026-11-09, https://github.com/alplabai/signex/issues/2199#issuecomment-6747372142; the name rule: maintainer, #2199 comment 2026-11-10, https://github.com/alplabai/signex/issues/2199#issuecomment-6761836527) | #2199 | `signex_net::join_rule::tests`, `signex_net::build::tests::wire_intent`, `signex_net::bus_ends::tests`, `signex_net::bus_intent::tests`, `signex_net::build::tests::placement_intent`, `signex_net::project::tests::name_changes`, `command::wire_run_tests`, `command::project_name_tests`, `command::sheet_entry_ref_tests`, `command::queries::pins_tests`, `catalog.golden.tsv` |

| 2026-11-09 | Cross probe between the schematic and the board (#1312). Tools ▸ Cross Select Mode is **on by default**: a selection on one side is selected in every sheet or board window on screen on the other side, never opening a document or taking focus, and an emptied selection empties the other side. Tools ▸ Cross Probe (`cross_probe`, new catalog row, no default key) brings the other side forward or opens it (the project's `pcb_file`, or the part's sheet from the hierarchy) and frames the match. A part is matched by sheet path + symbol UUID against the footprint's stored `schematic_link`, never by designator; a net by name. A selected pad stands for its net (its part when it has none), a track or via for its net. `toggle_cross_select_mode` (already in the catalog, Altium Ctrl+Shift+X) now runs and gains the menu label "Cross Select Mode". | Caner (cross-select default on; sheet-path + UUID link) | #1312 | `regression::cross_probe`, `signex_net::cross_probe::tests`, `state::cross_select::tests`, `menu_bar.golden.tsv`, `catalog.golden.tsv` |
| 2026-11-09 | PCB view controls (#2223): what a board view shows is session state in `UiState::pcb_layer_views`, one `PcbLayerView` per open board path, and is never written to the `.snxpcb` (saving it in the project is a follow-up). An entry is not dropped when its tab closes (reopening the board in the session restores its view), and Save As starts the new path with the default view. A view holds the hidden layers, one active copper layer and whether the ratsnest is drawn. A hidden layer's items are left out of the render snapshot and out of the selection index, so they are neither drawn nor hit (a selected item on a layer being hidden is deselected). A pad goes with its copper layers (an SMD pad on Top Layer, Top Solder and Top Paste is hidden with Top Layer; layer names as in the 2026-11-10 Altium layer-name row), a pad on no copper layer with its names, and a footprint body (#2240) with the layer of its side. Copper off the active layer is drawn at 0.35 alpha and the active layer is drawn last; an item spanning the active layer (a through via while an inner layer is active) counts as on it. Stepping skips hidden copper; hiding the active layer makes the next shown copper layer active, and with every copper layer hidden nothing is dimmed and the status pill is not shown. A layer name this build does not know cannot be hidden. Layer names are catalog messages (`pcb.ftl`), except a mechanical layer the user named. View commands act on the board of the window they come from: an undocked PCB window's own board, else the main window's active one; an undocked PCB window is enabled by its own board (`has_pcb`) and gets the `pcb` shortcut context from it. New commands: `toggle_ratsnest`, `next_layer` (`+`, `Shift++`, `*`, `Shift+*`), `prev_layer` (`-`), `toggle_layer_visibility` (the active layer), `show_all_layers`, all greyed without a PCB, and `toggle_layers_panel` (always enabled; the panel has an empty state). The keys are in a new `pcb` context of the Altium and Classic profiles only; the keymap parser reads `+` and `Shift++` as the plus key; the keymap golden writes the minus key as `"-"`. `toggle_ratsnest` has no default key. Behaviour changes: (1) View gains five board-view rows and a Layers panel row; (2) a PCB tab dims copper off its active layer (Top at first) where it drew every layer alike; (3) the Layers panel and a status-bar pill show the layers and the active one; (4) an undocked PCB window no longer gets the `pcb` shortcut context from the main window's board, only from its own. | Caner (approved on #2271, 2026-11-10) | #2223 | `regression::pcb_view`, `app::handlers::pcb_view::tests`, `renderer::pcb::layer_view::tests`, `pcb_canvas::select::tests`, `pcb_canvas::layer_style::tests`, `panels::pcb_layers::tests`, `keymap::binding::tests`, `menu_bar.golden.tsv`, `catalog.golden.tsv`, `builtin_profiles.golden.tsv` |

| 2026-11-10 | `place_component`, `place_sheet_symbol`, `place_sheet_entry` and `sync_sheet_pins` run from operands and leave `gui_only` (#2199 PR C); `place_symbol` and `create_sheet_from_sheet_symbol` stay `gui_only`. `place_component` places a library row (symbol, footprint and parameters together): `library` (an open library by id or `.snxlib` path) and `part` (a row id, else a part number; several matches are refused), `unit` placed separately, the units of one part sharing its designator. `place_sheet_symbol` refers to its child file and writes nothing; `place_sheet_entry` and `sync_sheet_pins` run from operands too. A placed part, sheet symbol or entry whose pin tip or entry lands on a wire end, junction, label or another pin is refused with the reason, following the PR B rule that an edit from operands never connects anything the caller did not name. Headless and agent edits do no file IO inside `update`: the reads their checks need (the project's closed sheets, a library row, a child file) run off the UI thread as a `Task`, the edit runs when they land and its outcome (CLI line, socket reply, `apply_edits` answer) is reported then; a target that changed meanwhile is refused; this covers the PR B checks too. Implementation choices (not in the decisions): the corners are `start` and `end`, the names `draw_rectangle` uses; `place_device_sheet_symbol` stays `gui_only`; a part number matches the internal or the manufacturer part number; `at` is a grid coordinate, never a pin reference; a given `designator` already in the project is accepted only for a unit of the same row placed on no sheet of the project; `file` is text checked inside the project folder as the Create Sheet dialog checks it; the connection rule also refuses a wire run and a sheet entry under the point, pin to pin included though the netlist joins nothing pin to pin, and applies to synced entries; a bus-named sheet entry on a bus (end or mid-span) is refused, while a pin tip or a plain-named entry on a bus connects nothing and is accepted; a pin tip or entry on a no-connect marker is refused; `place_sheet_symbol` refuses a `file` that is the edited sheet or one above it, the ancestors taken from the project as the edit reads it and from a window's hierarchy, and a `file` whose own subtree places the edited sheet; the nets a `place_sheet_symbol` file brings in are judged by the frozen name rule (a short or rename needs the new `allow_merge` operand; a file not there yet brings none), and `allow_merge` is also needed for a `file` that is a page of the project or already placed on another of its sheets, or that is or places an unreadable sheet (unknown names and subtree); a socket request waiting for its reads is refused when agent connections were turned off or its document left the agent's reach meanwhile; a body over a wire is not judged; the reads are planned from the operands (the union of an `apply_edits` list, read once, each edit planned on the sheet it acts on, a closed file a `place_sheet_symbol` of the list refers to read with the project as a child-to-be), the staleness check compares the revision of the target and of its project's open sheets and refuses a project sheet opened or closed meanwhile, a socket reply reports only what its own run changed and logged, the journal line of a socket or headless edit that waited is written when it runs, once, a waiting request refused at the gate when its reads land is an access-journal `refused` line as at request time, a reader that fails is said in Messages and refused without a retry, a link out of the project folder is checked off the UI thread too, a library path is compared lexically (no disk), and the menu's Sync Sheet Pins keeps its read. None of the four writes a file, so none is in `PROJECT_WIDE`; all four are `apply_edits` edits. | Maintainer (#2199 comments 2026-11-10: PR C, https://github.com/alplabai/signex/issues/2199#issuecomment-6769277106; the PR B rule and frozen name rule, https://github.com/alplabai/signex/issues/2199#issuecomment-6761836527; no file IO in `update`, https://github.com/alplabai/signex/issues/2199#issuecomment-6771692137) | #2199 | `signex_net::build::tests::placement_contacts`, `command::part_operand_tests`, `command::hierarchy_operand_tests`, `command::edit_read_tests`, `command::edit_read_list_tests`, `command::sheet_symbol_check_tests`, `agent::tests::deferred`, `agent::tests::journal::a_headless_edit_that_waited_for_its_reads_is_journaled_once`, `command::project_name_tests::a_headless_wire_that_waited_for_the_project_is_journaled_once`, `designator_scan_tests::an_agent_part_never_supersedes_the_scan_a_drop_waits_on`, `agent_edits::wire::tests`, `signex-cli` `tests/place_part.rs`, `catalog.golden.tsv` |

| 2026-11-09 | Board sketch, domain half (#2234). A board stores its sketch and what it baked (keepouts, cutouts, mounting holes, V-scores) in `PcbBoard::sketch`; the baked outline is the board's own `outline`. `BoardCommand::SetBoardSketch` is the one way any of them changes: one undo step, bit-exact undo, checked by `check_batch`; `SetBoardOutline` is refused while the sketch owns the outline. The `.snxpcb` gains an optional `[board_sketch]` section (sketch, `[[keepouts]]`, `[[cutouts]]`, `[[v_scores]]`, holes as a nm TSV block `[board_sketch.holes]`), written only when the board has a sketch, so a board without one re-saves byte-identical; the format token stays `snxpcb/1.3`. The board bake refuses (typed `BoardBakeError`) where the footprint bake warns. A mounting hole names its net by name, resolved to the number at bake. DRC reports a `Keepout` violation, sorted after the other kinds. See `docs/PCB_SKETCH.md` | Caner | #2234 | `format::board_sketch_tests`, `pcb::board_sketch_tests`, `board::tests` (signex-bake), `drc::keepout_tests` |

| 2026-11-10 | Board sketch mode (#2234, step 3). `board_sketch` (new catalog row, PCB, needs a PCB, no default key) enters sketch mode on the main window's board or leaves it; it is Design ▸ Board Sketch, the PCB Active Bar's one button (new: the PCB view had no Active Bar), a board canvas menu row and a palette row. The mode reuses the footprint editor's sketch tool unchanged by hosting a `FootprintDocument` that lives only in the session, keyed by the board path, so its canvas, sketch Active Bar, DOF chip and row, snap glyphs, solver, off-thread solve and Properties handlers all run on it; with `board_backdrop` set the hosting editor shows the sketch tools only, draws the board under the sketch and never runs the footprint bake. Properties gains a Board section (outline, mounting hole drill / plated / net); keepouts and cutouts use the existing Role picker. While the mode is on, Undo and Redo step the working copy, not the board, and nothing is written. Leave, Esc (with no tool armed) and `board_sketch` commit a changed copy then leave, Apply commits and stays, Discard drops it; a commit is `bake_board` plus one `SetBoardSketch` step, and a refused bake shows its error on the notice card, in Messages and in the mode's bar, leaving the board unchanged and the mode on. One board at a time, main window only: undocking the board applies the sketch and leaves the mode, and the mode cannot start on an undocked board. Save, Save All, quit, Close Project and closing the board's tab first apply a live sketch as one undoable board edit (Save and Save All keep the mode on), and a live sketch counts as unsaved work; a refused bake holds that save, close or quit and says why. Only History restore, a recovery read, Discard All in the close prompt (`close_tab_now`) and parking a closed board (`pcb_parking.rs`) drop the copy, each with a Messages line naming the board (changed on #2271 from 'closing discards the copy', a data-loss finding by Hakan). The PCB canvas now draws the board outline (it drew none before), cutouts and holes as openings and keepouts hatched. | Caner (approved on #2271, 2026-11-10) | #2234 | `regression::board_sketch`, `view::context_menu::tests::board_sketch_row_runs_the_command`, `view::board_sketch::tests`, `renderer::pcb::board_features::tests`, `menu_bar.golden.tsv`, `catalog.golden.tsv`, `builtin_profiles.golden.tsv` |

| 2026-11-11 | UI wiring of the `signex-i18n` library (#2063; the PR says `Refs`, the issue stays open until the `tr` catalog, #2065). Every search surface (command palette, Parameter Manager, components panel, library browser, component and primitive pickers, library panel, projects panel, Preferences keyboard search) folds query and text with the one `fold_for_search` / per-char `fold_char_for_search`, which follow the MESSAGE locale; user content (MPN, manufacturer, parameter values, file names) is folded the same way until #2065 revisits it (2026-11-10). The per-char fold keeps `İ` to `i` under every non-Turkic locale, so `en-US` palette matching of `İ` is unchanged (2026-11-11); the string-fold surfaces gain full-fold matches under `en-US` (`ss` finds `ß`, ligatures, final sigma) and treat `İ` as trunk's `to_lowercase` did. Library browser text columns sort by the formatting locale's collator with natural digit runs, their keys case-folded by that same locale (`fold_for_sort`, so `IŞIK` sorts in the `ı` group under `tr-TR`; review 2026-11-11); Preferences keyboard search folds key names and command ids without the Turkic rules (they are not language text, so `ins` keeps finding `INS`); the price column and the BOM table stay locale-free (the BOM's on-screen order must match the export, `designator_sort_matches_the_export_order`). (2) Tools ▸ Re-Annotate Board... (`reannotate_board`, new catalog row, no default key), placed after Design Rule Check with the other board rows, opens Positional Re-Annotate: by X then by Y (default) or by Y then by X, an exact sort at 1 µm with no column or row tolerance; bottom side after the top side (default) or with it; numbered from 1 within each prefix, prefix kept, surrounding spaces trimmed and only ASCII digits read as the number; locked footprints and footprints with no schematic link keep their designators and numbers (Update PCB matches an unlinked footprint by designator, so renumbering it would swap parts; hkngln review); the options are remembered until the app quits; Execute is one labelled board undo step at the list's revision. The dialog and the Properties section say that Update PCB renames parts back and Back Annotate keeps them; the Update PCB rows stay ticked (the command picks the direction). (3) Back Annotate (PR 2): the command picks the direction, the board wins for each ticked row; unannotated or duplicate schematic designators block their rows; Tools ▸ Annotation ▸ Back Annotate Schematics... stays and Design ▸ Update Schematics in `<project>` is added; closed sheets are edited in memory; on a reused child sheet a value row is read-only and a designator row writes that placement only. | 2026-11-11 | ERC on demand (#2199 step D). The maintainer decided: (1) a new read-only query `check_erc document=<sheet> [baseline=<run>]` checks the project of the named sheet (a loose sheet on its own) as ERC does today (same rules, rule set, pin matrix, `erc.dsl`, waivers, project-wide passes), closed sheets read off the UI thread, answers with data (`Answered`) and writes nothing to the ERC panel, the ERC state or Messages; `run_erc` is unchanged; (2) one check-result shape in `signex-types` (`signex_types::check`): a report `{check, run, findings, waived, ...}`, each finding `{rule, severity, message, evidence, items, sheet, key}`, `key` stable across runs and derived from the waiver key; DRC adopts it next, the #2245 requirement checks after; (3) each answer carries a `run` id, the app keeps the last few runs in memory, and `baseline=<run>` adds `new` (keys not in that run) and `gone` (keys only in that run); (4) waived findings are not in `findings` but in `waived`, each with the waiver that hides it, and lapsed waivers are reported. Implementation choices (not in the decision): the report also has `lapsed` (the per-finding waivers whose finding changed) and `warnings` (the warnings and errors Run ERC's run says in Messages, in its words: an unreadable sheet, an `erc.dsl` that does not compile, a broken hierarchy, a child reference leaving its folder, an unreadable stored rule set, an unrecognised rule entry; not its info lines, and not the rule-profile drift check Run ERC starts beside its run, "Cannot check this project's rule profile for changes: ...", which `check_erc` does not run), `rule_set` (a check-generic optional field, for ERC `{source: project|machine, profile, sha256}`), and `baseline` only when one was asked; `key` is the first 128 bits (32 hex) of a SHA-256 over the sheet path, the rule id and the waiver identity objects (the fingerprint left out, so moving or editing the object keeps the key), or over the sheet path, rule id and message for a finding that names no object, with `#2`, `#3` added to a repeat; findings are ordered by sheet path, then error/warning/info, rule, message, key and evidence, so a report is deterministic whatever order ERC emitted; severity reuses `signex_types::violation::Severity`; ERC's own facts go in `evidence` (`rule_name`, `space`, `at`, `child_sheet`, `net`, `pin`, `waiver_key`); a waiver object carries `kind` (`finding` with the stored waiver's fields, or `net` with `rule_id` and `net`), and the findings a net waiver hides are listed one by one (the job now keeps them, `signex_erc::waivers::partition_waived`; the panel's counts are unchanged); `sheet` is the sheet's path; the app keeps 8 runs (`KEPT_CHECK_RUNS`) in process memory, numbered from 1, a refused answer is not numbered or kept, and a baseline that is unknown, no longer kept, or a run of another check or project (scope: the project file, or the loose sheet) is refused with the reason, checked when the run lands; the run checks only the project's sheets (no loose tabs outside it, unlike Run ERC); the rule set is the project's stored one or else this machine's preferences, said in `warnings` and `rule_set`, never in Messages; for a sheet of a project, in a headless run or for an agent, the run reads from disk only what a headless query may read (`document_ids::confined_read`: inside the project folder, links followed, a regular file under the size cap), for pages, root, children and `erc.dsl` alike, and names a file it does not read in `warnings`; a loose sheet in a headless run is not confined (no project folder; the agent channel cannot reach it), the check-then-read gap of the headless queries applies too, and Run ERC's reads are not limited; the request is deferred like an edit waiting for its reads (the shared ticket, `settle_deferred` for a headless run, one socket reply when the run lands, the agent gate checked again then) and, as a query, writes no edit-journal line; on the agent channel a loose sheet is refused as for every query naming a document; a palette or key call is refused as for every query. | Maintainer (#2199 comment 2026-11-11, https://github.com/alplabai/signex/issues/2199#issuecomment-6798772157; no file IO in `update`, https://github.com/alplabai/signex/issues/2199#issuecomment-6771692137) | #2199 | `signex_types::check::tests`, `signex_erc::check::tests`, `signex_erc::waivers::tests::a_partition_keeps_each_hidden_finding_with_its_waiver_in_order`, `state::check_runs::tests`, `command::queries::check_erc_tests`, `agent::tests::check_erc`, `agent::allow::tests`, `signex-cli` `tests/check_erc.rs`, `catalog.golden.tsv` |

| 2026-11-12 | DRC rules on the #1085 layers, step 2a (#2307, decision https://github.com/alplabai/signex/issues/2307#issuecomment-6819660306). **Maintainer decisions:** "DRC rules live inside the rule layers, not in a loose file: the DSL text sits in the profile (`.snxrules`) and in the project's `rules.drc` section, so it is covered by the rule-set hash, the profile and the drift check" (comment 6819660306, point 2); for step 2a, covering the `drc` section by the rule-set hash, the drift check, Update from profile and Save as profile moves to step 2b with the rule language, so in 2a the hash stays ERC-only, every existing hash is unchanged, and the `drc` section is stored, round-tripped and resolved for severities only (https://github.com/alplabai/signex/issues/2307#issuecomment-6824136804); the plan of step 2a: DRC rule ids, built-in default severities with short, clearance and keepout errors and unconnected a warning, and the `rules.drc` section, with no result change; the rule language (`signex-drc-dsl`) and its v1 rule kinds are step 2b; precedence is "most specific wins" (between rules of the rule language, step 2b). **Implementation choices in this step:** the rule layers apply in order, a later layer overriding an earlier one (built-in, profile, project); rule ids `builtin::short`, `builtin::clearance`, `builtin::unconnected`, `builtin::keepout` (`ViolationKind::rule_id`); the `drc` section is `DrcRuleSet` in `signex-types` (`severity` keyed by the full rule id, `dsl` kept but not read, unknown keys kept), added to `ProjectRules` and `RuleProfile` without a schema or format-token bump; the resolved table travels in `DrcRules::severities` and `DrcReport::severity_of` is the per-violation lookup (no field on `Violation`); `off` removes a rule's severity but never filters a violation; the shipped `Signex Default` profile gains no `drc` section, so its version and hash stay; `drc` has its own `DrcRuleSet::sha256_hex`, which nothing records until 2b; the app uses a project's own `rules.drc` when it stores a `rules` section, else the machine's `User Default` profile, as ERC does; a new project copies its seed profile's `drc`, and "Store ERC rules in this project" and a first ERC waiver copy the machine's `User Default` `drc`, so starting to store rules never changes the DRC layer; Duplicate in Preferences ▸ Rule Profiles copies the whole profile, `drc` and unknown keys included (and its `schema` key as written); Save as profile over an existing profile still replaces that file with the project's `erc` only, so the target's `drc` is dropped until step 2b, as on trunk; the machine-rules warning now reads "The machine's rules (<file>) could not be read", since the file holds DRC rules too, and the first-waiver line now says the project "now stores its own rules" (was "its own ERC rules"). Behaviour change: `drc` was a reserved key carried verbatim; it is now typed, so a `drc` of another shape makes the project's whole `rules` section unreadable (#1180: bytes kept, ERC on the machine rules, rule edits and waivers refused) and refuses a `.snxrules` profile (file kept, never overwritten), keeping the rule that a value's shape changes only with a schema bump. Run DRC results, the DRC panel and its Messages lines are unchanged for projects and profiles without a `drc` section. See `docs/architecture/pcb-drc.md` | Hakan (#2307, 2026-11-12) | #2307 | `signex-drc` `severity_tests::*`; `signex-types` `project::rules::tests::{the_drc_section_is_read_and_round_trips_with_unknown_keys, a_drc_key_from_before_the_section_existed_is_kept, a_rule_set_without_drc_loads_empty_and_resaves_byte_identical, a_drc_section_holding_only_rule_text_is_kept}`, `project::rule_profile::tests::the_drc_section_does_not_enter_the_profile_content_hash`; `signex-erc` `project_rules::profile::tests::builtin_profile_hash_and_bytes_are_unchanged_by_the_drc_section`; `app::handlers::pcb_drc_tests::*`; `regression::pcb_drc::a_run_carries_the_severities_of_the_machine_profile_and_finds_the_same`; `project::rules::tests::a_malformed_drc_section_is_kept_and_marks_the_rules_unreadable`, `project::rule_profile::tests::a_profile_with_a_malformed_drc_section_is_refused`, `rule_profiles::manage_tests::{a_user_default_with_a_malformed_drc_section_is_never_overwritten, duplicating_a_profile_copies_its_drc_section}` |

| 2026-11-12 | #2232 setup dialogs, second review follow-up (PR #2317). Profile edits of `setup_profiles` run in the order they were made: each takes a ticket on the UI thread and its worker waits for every earlier edit of that key (`fonts::merge_queue`), so Save then Save or Remove of one profile lands in order whatever the disk speed. A `setup_profiles` value that is not a list refuses the edit and is left as it is. A new profile's id is numbered past every id already in the file (unreadable entries and other instances' profiles included) and never replaces one; only a re-save of a profile this instance holds replaces its entry. A write that fails takes the in-memory change back and says so. New Schematic on a project that stores no sheet defaults (and names no saved profile) starts from the blank sheet Add New always made (A4), so an unchanged Create writes the same sheet as before; New Project's own default stays A3. The New Schematic dialog applies a designator standard only when the user picked one there, or picked a saved profile while the project stored none; headless `new_schematic_sheet` the same way for a named saved profile. Remove from the New PCB / New Schematic dialogs un-names the project's default profile as the Project tab does (one undoable project edit). A failed headless sheet-name search is said in Messages. | 2026-11-13 | The DRC rule language, step 2b-1 (#2307). **Maintainer decisions** (https://github.com/alplabai/signex/issues/2307#issuecomment-6819660306, https://github.com/alplabai/signex/issues/2307#issuecomment-6837552967): a sibling crate `signex-drc-dsl` in the family and parser style of `signex-erc-dsl`, which is not touched; the rule text lives in the rule layers (profile `drc.dsl`, project `rules.drc.dsl`), not a loose file; v1 rule kinds are clearance scoped by net, net class, net pattern and layer, track width min/max, via diameter min and drill min; precedence is specificity first and strictness only between equals (net > net class > net pattern > layer > board-wide), a more specific rule applying even when looser; today's board setup clearance and net-class values stay the lowest, implicit rules so existing boards give the same results; the v1 grammar already carries object pairs for clearance (track, pad, via, hole, board edge) and an inner/outer layer predicate, and leaves room for a region scope (v2) without a language change; v1 checks only what DRC measures. **Implementation choices:** the grammar `rule <name> on clearance\|track\|via [between <obj> and <obj>] [when <cond> and ...] require <limit> and ... then error\|warning\|info ["message"]`, conditions `net ==`, `class ==`, `net matches` (net-class glob), `layer == inner\|outer\|<layer name>`, a region being one more condition subject (reported "not supported yet"); lengths with `mm`, `mil`, `um`/`µm`, `nm` attached to the number or after spaces (after a space only those words count as a unit), to integer nm via `pcb_geom::mm_to_nm`, no arithmetic; a limit of 0 is a warning (a minimum of 0 checks nothing, `width <= 0mm` reports every track), a clearance over 10 mm a warning and over 100 mm refused (a clearance widens the search around the items it covers); a syntax error applies no rule of that text, an invalid rule or a second valid rule of one name is dropped alone (an invalid rule does not take its name), a `hole` or `edge` pair compiles but is not applied, each said in Messages with layer, line and column, and any rule text not applied is also said where results are read (a short Messages warning line of its own, `DRC rules: N written rule(s) not applied` or `DRC rules: the rule text was not applied (syntax error)`, apart from the summary so the 160-byte Messages cap never cuts it, and a warning line in the DRC panel), only when rule text is present; `signex-drc` owns the rule model (`UserRule`, `NetScope`, `Limit`, `CopperObject`) and the precedence, `signex-drc-dsl` only the language; the implicit rules form one tier resolved as before (the largest of setup and class values), below a written board-wide rule; specificity is the net-scope rank (net, class, pattern, every net), then the number of narrowing conditions a rule adds (a layer condition, an object pair), so a layer-only rule ranks above a board-wide one and `class == "HV" and layer == inner` above `class == "HV"`; specificity is per object: each side of a clearance pair is resolved for its own net (its most specific written rule, else its implicit value) and the pair needs the stricter of its two sides, so a looser rule for one net never relaxes the other net's rule, as the implicit tier always combined two class values: a rule for one net can loosen that net's own class value but never the other net's, and a rule for one net below the setup value loosens no pair (this per-side reading of 6837552967 §1 is pending the maintainer's confirmation); a layer condition and an object pair count the same; a clearance is resolved per shared copper layer and the pair needs the most of its layers; equal specificity and value go to the first written rule; when the two sides of a pair ask the same value the more severe side wins (a milder rule never downgrades the other side's finding) and at equal severity a written rule is named over the implicit value; a written rule that wins for a net of a class below what the net would otherwise need (the larger of setup and class values) is applied and said, once per rule and class, as a Messages warning (`DRC rule 'base' lowers net class 'HV' clearance 0.8 → 0.15 mm`), never silently, and naming the board setup clearance when that is the larger, checked for each object pair a rule names; written rules are resolved only on the board's own copper layers (none listed: top and bottom), a through via or pad's all-layer mask clipped to them, a clearance per shared layer and a track or via limit per layer the object reaches with the strictest layer's limit holding; a caller that never resolves severities still gets each written rule's `then` severity from the report; a rule whose scope covers no net is said: a net class that does not exist as a warning, a net or pattern the board lacks or a class without nets on it as info (a shared profile may name nets a board lacks); the broad-phase search around an item reaches the widest clearance any pair with its net can need (implicit, or the widest written rule covering that net); with a written clearance rule the default-clearance note and the summary say the board value holds only where no written rule applies; rule ids `user::<name>`, the rule's `then` severity overridable by a `drc.severity` entry for that id (later layer wins, `off` included, as for built-ins), an entry for a `user::` id no rule has stays unrecognised, and as for built-ins a severity is recorded on the report but not yet shown and `off` hides nothing; new kinds `TrackWidth`, `ViaDiameter`, `ViaDrill` (`builtin::track_width`, `builtin::via_diameter`, `builtin::via_drill`, built-in error) after the old ones, and `Violation::rule` (last field) naming the written rule; width and via limits are checked only when a written rule sets them, the board setup's `track_min_width`, `via_min_diameter`, `via_min_drill` and the net classes' routing values stay unchecked (enforcing them changes existing boards' results and is a separate decision); the app compiles the same layer choice as the severities (project `rules.drc` when it stores rules, else the machine `User Default`) inside the `spawn_blocking` job; a project rule replaces a profile rule of the same name; the language has no comments yet. Hash, drift check, Update from profile and Save as profile for `drc` stay step 2b-2. With no rule text the results, their order, the DRC panel and its Messages are unchanged. Behaviour changes: `dsl` text stored under 2a (in a profile or a project's `rules.drc`) is now compiled and applied by Run DRC; `drc.severity` entries for `builtin::track_width`, `builtin::via_diameter` and `builtin::via_drill` are now recognised instead of listed as unrecognised. Known limit: the panel summary counts neither keepout (#2300) nor width and via findings, which are listed as rows. See `docs/architecture/pcb-drc.md`, `docs/DRC_RULES.md` | Maintainer (#2307, 2026-11-12 and 2026-11-13) | #2307 | `signex-drc-dsl` `tests::*`, `doc_examples` (reads `docs/DRC_RULES.md`); `signex-drc` `user_rules_tests::*`, `severity_tests::*`, `real_boards`; `pcb-geom` `ratsnest_real_boards`; `signex-types` `project::rules::tests::multi_line_rule_text_round_trips_unchanged`; `panels::drc::tests::{a_size_row_says_the_limit_it_breaks, the_default_clearance_note_follows_whether_written_rules_ran}`; `app::handlers::pcb_drc_tests::a_run_says_the_text_messages_then_the_classes_a_rule_lowers`; `regression::pcb_drc::{without_rule_text_the_report_and_its_messages_are_unchanged, rule_text_in_the_profile_is_applied_by_the_run, a_rule_that_does_not_compile_is_reported_and_skipped, rule_text_with_a_syntax_error_is_said_beside_the_results, a_rule_that_matches_no_net_is_said, rule_text_without_a_clearance_rule_keeps_the_clearance_wording}` |
| 2026-11-12 | The PCB board view turns on screen (#2316): 90°, 180° and any angle, clockwise positive, kept in `[0, 360)`. The turn is view state in the board canvas's camera (`pcb_canvas::BoardCamera`), beside pan and zoom, with their lifetime: each window's canvas has its own, a tab switch in the main window keeps it, a board opened into a tab starts at 0° and an undocked window starts at 0°. Never a `BoardCommand`: no undo step, no dirty mark, nothing written to the `.snxpcb`. One transform, `screen = turn(board) × scale + offset` (`signex_gfx::view_rotation::ViewRotation`), is read by the CPU draw, the GPU shader camera (`CameraUniform::ortho_turned`), upload and CPU culling, picking, the highlight layer, the grid (its lines stay on board multiples of the pitch and turn with the board), cursor readout, drags and Fit (it frames the board's turned box). Snapping stays on the board grid. A box is dragged along the screen: on a turned view it selects by the turned rectangle (`pcb_geom::turned_marquee_select`), and left to right on screen is the window. PCB scenes draw no text today, so no text-upright rule is needed yet. New commands: `view_rotate` (optional `degrees`, any angle within ±360, default 90; runs from operands for agents), `view_rotate_ccw`, `view_rotate_180`, `view_rotate_reset`, all greyed without a board (Reset also at 0°) and with no default keys (no free conventional chord). Behaviour changes: (1) View gains four rotation rows after Show All Layers; (2) the PCB Active Bar gains a Rotate View button after Board Sketch; (3) the status bar of a board shows a View angle readout before the zoom, whose click opens a field for any angle (Enter applies a number, other text keeps the field open, Esc or a second click closes it, and it closes when the view turns by another route or the tab leaves the board); (4) `document_info` on a board answers `view_rotation_deg` (`null` while no window shows it); (5) `BoxSelected` carries `view_rotation_deg`; (6) a new operand kind `turn` (the `degrees` operand). | Caner (#2316, 2026-11-12) | #2316 | `regression::pcb_view_rotation`, `pcb_canvas::camera::tests`, `pcb_canvas::cull::tests::a_turned_view_culls_by_the_box_around_it`, `scene_gpu_upload::tests::a_turned_view_culls_by_the_box_around_it`, `signex_gfx::view_rotation::tests`, `signex_gfx::camera::tests`, `pcb_geom::turned_marquee::tests`, `app::command::operand_handler_tests::view_rotate_by_operands_is_the_view_row`, `view::board_sketch::tests`, `menu_bar.golden.tsv`, `catalog.golden.tsv` |
| 2026-11-13 | Rule profiles cover the `drc` section, step 2b-2 (#2307). **Maintainer decision** (https://github.com/alplabai/signex/issues/2307#issuecomment-6824136804, restated in the plan of 6837552967): covering the `drc` section by the rule-set hash, the drift check, Update from profile and Save as profile is done in step 2b, with the rule language; it follows point 2 of 6819660306 ("so it is covered by the rule-set hash, the profile and the drift check"). **Implementation choices:** one hash, `signex_types::project::rule_set_sha256(erc, drc)`, used by `RuleProfile::content_sha256` and recorded in the existing `rules.profile.sha256`: while `drc` is empty it is `ErcRules::sha256_hex` exactly, so every profile and project without `drc` keeps its hash (the shipped `Signex Default` stays `89b152e9…dd76`) and no format token, key or schema changes; a non-empty `drc` makes it the SHA-256 of `{"erc": .., "drc": ..}` (severities, rule text and foreign keys all count); no separate DRC hash field is recorded; drift is unchanged code (`has_drifted` compares the recorded hash with the profile's), so a DRC-only profile change is drift; the ERC report's "(modified)" label compares the recorded hash with the project's `rule_set_sha256`, while the report header and CSV row keep the ERC hash, so ERC report bytes only change in the label and only for a project with `drc`; the DRC half of adopt and diff lives in `signex-drc` (`profile` module: `adopt_profile_drc`, `diff_drc_against_profile`, `DrcRuleChange`), the ERC half stays in `signex-erc`, and the app's rule-profile handlers call both (no ERC code moved); adopt takes the profile's `drc` and keeps a project severity id that is neither a built-in DRC id nor `user::`, plus foreign keys only the project holds, while `user::` severities follow the replaced rule text; the confirmation lists DRC lines after the ERC lines (`DRC severity <id>: <from> -> <to>` with `default` for an absent entry, `DRC rule text: changed (N lines -> M lines)` / `added (…)` / `removed (…)`, `DRC other setting <key>: …`), and Apply re-checks both diffs; Save as profile writes the project's `erc` and `drc` (`rule_profiles::profile_of_project_rules`), not its waivers, over an existing profile too; "Store ERC rules in this project" records the shipped profile only when its ERC and the machine's DRC section together equal it, so with machine DRC rules it records no provenance. Behaviour changes: (1) a profile with a non-empty `drc` gets a new hash, so a project recorded against it by a 2a or 2b-1 build gets the one-time drift note, its ERC report names the profile `<profile> (modified)`, and Update from profile shows the comparison with no listed lines when the rules are equal; Apply changes no rule and records the new hash; (2) a project whose own `drc` differs from its recorded profile's is named "(modified)" in the ERC report; (3) Update from profile now replaces the project's `drc`, so a project with `drc` whose profile has none loses it on Apply, after the confirmation lists each removed entry; (4) Save as profile no longer drops a target profile's `drc` and writes the project's; (5) with machine DRC rules, storing rules records no shipped provenance; (6) two Update-from-profile Messages lines now say "rules" instead of "ERC rules" for every project ("Project rules already match profile …", "… the project's rules changed after the differences were shown"), since the update covers both sections. A `user::` severity entry that goes away reads `the rule's own` (its `then` severity) instead of `default`. The combined hash form is pinned byte for byte. Projects and profiles without `drc`: every hash, provenance record, drift result, Update diff, Save as bytes, ERC behaviour and Run DRC output unchanged. The 2a test `project::rule_profile::tests::the_drc_section_does_not_enter_the_profile_content_hash` pinned the superseded 2a choice and is replaced by the first two `signex-types` tests below. See `docs/architecture/snxrules-format.md` ("What the hash covers"), `docs/architecture/pcb-drc.md` ("Rule profiles") | Hakan (#2307, 2026-11-12) | #2307 | `signex-types` `project::rule_profile::tests::{a_profile_without_drc_hashes_as_its_erc_section, the_drc_section_enters_the_profile_content_hash, the_erc_and_drc_hash_form_is_pinned}`; `signex-erc` `project_rules::profile::tests::{builtin_profile_hash_and_bytes_are_unchanged_by_the_drc_section, a_drc_only_profile_change_is_drift}`; `signex-drc` `profile_tests::*`; `app::handlers::erc::rule_profiles_drc_tests::*`; `rule_profiles::manage_tests::save_as_carries_the_project_drc_section_through_an_overwrite` |
| 2026-11-13 | Update PCB never ticks the removal of a footprint whose part is still in the design (#2318). Matching is by link; an unlinked footprint is linked by a unique designator on every Update PCB run (a Link row, unchanged). (1) A footprint whose stored link names no part of the design (its sheet path changed, for example a sheet symbol re-created) is relinked, sheet ignored, to the one unmatched on-board part that shares at least one unit uuid with its link: a Link row (the new sheet path) and no Add row, and the designator, value and footprint rows follow as for any matched footprint. It is never matched by designator, so a deleted part replaced by an unrelated one under the same designator stays a Remove plus an Add. With no such part, or several (a child sheet placed twice, or a multi-unit part whose units now sit in separate parts), the Remove row stays ticked as before. A link under an unreadable sheet is left alone. Known limit, unchanged: a re-created sheet whose new child sheet is unreadable lists ticked removals. (2) A footprint whose link names a part another footprint holds, or whose stale link shares a unit uuid with a part that was relinked or matched by designator in this run, when that part is on the board, gets a Remove row that is listed but not ticked, with the note "R is linked to P too, which another footprint holds; removing it is listed but not ticked" (`EcoNote::ExtraLink`), the Update PCB counterpart of Back Annotate's `BackNote::ExtraLink` (row above). A part held through its own intact link does not count for a stale link, so the deleted instance of a reused child sheet keeps its ticked Remove. Known limit: if a reused sheet's instance is deleted while another instance is re-created in the same run, a footprint of the deleted instance can take the re-created part's relink (a ticked Link and rename) when it comes first on the board, and the re-created instance's own footprint is then listed as an unticked Remove; no footprint is removed by default. Check the Link and rename rows in that case. The "link names this part" test (same sheet path, a shared unit) is `SymbolLink::names_part`, used by Update PCB, Back Annotate, net sync, link by designator and cross-probe. Behaviour change: both cases used to list a ticked removal. See `docs/architecture/adr-0005-integer-nm-board-geometry.md` section 11 | Hakan (#2318, 2026-11-13) | #2318 | `signex-engine` `pcb::eco::relink_tests::*` |
| 2026-11-13 | DRC on demand, `check_drc` (#2199 step 3, after #2307). **Maintainer decisions** (https://github.com/alplabai/signex/issues/2199#issuecomment-6817821301 points 1, 3 and 4; point 2 replaced by https://github.com/alplabai/signex/issues/2199#issuecomment-6818011610; the shared check shape of https://github.com/alplabai/signex/issues/2199#issuecomment-6798772157; no file IO in `update` for headless and agent paths, https://github.com/alplabai/signex/issues/2199#issuecomment-6771692137): a read-only query `check_drc document=<board> [baseline=<run>]` checks one `.snxpcb`, an open board from memory and a closed one read off the UI thread within the headless read limits; it answers in the `signex_types::check` shape, writes nothing to the DRC panel, the DRC state or Messages, and `run_drc` is unchanged; a board in a project takes its net classes from that project, a board with no project is checked with its own setup rules and the report says so in `warnings`; the baseline is scoped to the board file; severities come from the #2307 rule model's layers (built-in, profile, project, a written rule's own severity); `waived` and `lapsed` stay empty (no DRC waivers yet; superseded by the 2026-11-17 DRC waivers row: they hold the board-file waivers); items the run could not check are `warnings`, and keepouts #2300 drops without a note are a documented limit. **Implementation choices:** the adapter is `signex_drc::findings::check_report` (domain): rule `builtin::<kind>` or the written rule's `user::<name>`, severity `DrcReport::severity_of`, a finding of a rule switched `off` not listed (as ERC drops a rule that is off); message in Run DRC's Messages words; evidence `nets` and `layers` by name, `at` in mm, `measured_nm`, `required_nm`, `keepout`, `rule_source` (`written`, `net_class`, `board_setup`, `default`); `items` are item uuids, a pad as `<footprint>/<pad>`; `sheet` is the board path; `key` the first 32 hex of SHA-256 over the board path, rule id, for a written rule also the violation kind (one rule can set several limits; a built-in rule's id is its kind, so its keys stay as they were, pinned by a test), items and keepout, so a move keeps it, and over the message for a finding that names no item or keepout (as ERC keys one). The board path is the document entry's own (the tab's path when open, the project folder joined with `pcb_file` when closed); another spelling of `document=` resolves to the same entry and keeps the keys and the baseline, but a `pcb_file` spelled differently from the tab path (case, `sub/../`) can change keys between an open and a closed check; `warnings` are the loose-board note, then what Run DRC says of its rules in Messages (unrecognised severity entries, the rule text's notes and remarks, rule text and rules not applied, a default clearance), then one per skipped item; `rule_set` is `source` (`project`/`machine`), `profile` (as the ERC report names it), `sha256` of the `drc` section used, `clearance_nm`, `from_default`. The run reuses `pcb_drc::run_check` and `drc_layers` (the rules engine and the layer choice Run DRC makes). A board's project is its own: the one its document entry names, else the open project whose `pcb_file` is the board (an open board's entry names no project: ownership follows listed sheets); with none, this machine's layer and the loose-board warning. Run DRC takes the active (sticky) project instead (`pcb_drc.rs`, `active_document_project`), so the two differ for a loose board while a project is active, or for a board of a project that is not the active one (#2332). An open board is checked from memory in the app's own process and in headless runs; over the agent channel a board open in the app is refused at the agent gate ("<board> is not part of a project open in this app") until #2331. The check plumbing is shared, not copied: `command::queries::check_run` (request, `start_check`, `check_answer` keyed by check id and scope), `AgentMsg::CheckFinished` for both checks, and the quiet header read keyed on "a check" (`queries::is_check`); `check_erc`'s replies and tests are unchanged. See `docs/CLI.md`, "Checks on demand" | Hakan (#2199, 2026-11-12) | #2199 | `signex-drc` `findings_tests::*`; `app::command::queries::check_drc_tests::*`; `app::agent::tests::check_drc::*` (`a_check_drc_on_a_board_open_in_the_app_is_refused_until_2331` pins the #2331 limit); `signex-cli` `tests/check_drc.rs` |
| 2026-11-14 | Fast grid 1 and Fast grid 2 are a Preferences setting (#1672): two mm fields in Preferences ▸ Theme ▸ Schematic Editor, defaults 1.27 mm (50 mil) and 2.54 mm (100 mil), user-editable and saved in `prefs.json` (`fast_grid_1_mm`, `fast_grid_2_mm`). `fast_grid_1`, `fast_grid_2` and `cycle_fast_grid` read the saved values; `canvas::grid::FAST_GRIDS_MM` is only the default. | 2026-11-15 | Polygon pours (#2216; the maintainer's brief of 2026-11-15: compute the fill, recompute it when the board changes, decide storage minimally). A pour is a zone with `fill_type = "pour"`; its copper is computed by `pcb_geom::zone_fills`: the outline less its holes, less every pad, track and via of another net (no net counts as another net) and every solid copper zone of another net on its layer grown by the clearance (the zone's, else `PcbSetup::clearance`, else 0.2 mm), less the keepouts on the layer, with parts thinner than `min_thickness` removed. Same-net copper is filled over solidly: no thermal relief. Not done: pour-to-pour clearance and `priority`, island removal, clipping to the board outline. The fill is **not saved**; it is recomputed from the saved outline and settings, once per board revision with the ratsnest, which reads it (a pad in a hole, in the clearance band or on another piece of the pour gets a line; DRC's `Unconnected` with it). This defers the router plan's stored, fill-on-demand pour to a later decision. The canvas draws the pieces as copper and strokes the outline, on both render paths. Fill and Solid Region zones stay solid copper, now less their holes for the ratsnest. Polygon Pour on a board is live: `place_board_polygon_pour` (default profile `P G` in the `pcb` context), drawn like a Solid Region on the active copper layer, on the net under its first corner, one undo step (`AddItems`); a pour's clearance is stored as 0 (follows the board). Behaviour in `docs/PCB_TOOLS.md`, "Polygon Pour". | Caner | #2216, #2373 | `pcb_geom` `zone_fill::tests`, `ratsnest::tests::fill`, `area_touch::tests`, `signex_renderer` `pcb::zone_fill::tests`, `signex_engine::pcb::draw::tests::a_pour_is_a_pour_zone_on_the_net_under_its_first_point_in_one_step`, `handlers::pcb_tool::area_tests::a_polygon_pour_fills_clear_of_other_nets_and_is_drawn_as_its_fill`, `menu_bar_place_board.golden.tsv` |

View ▸ 3D View (`view_board_3d`, `3` on a board in both built-in profiles, the palette) shows the board in view in 3D in the main window, or flat again; undocked board windows stay flat. | 2026-11-16 | Track and via locks, the via span (#2407). Tracks and vias carry a lock (`Segment::locked`, `Via::locked`) in an optional `locked` TSV column; `BoardCommand::SetItemsLocked` sets it as one step (footprints keep `SetFootprintLocked`). A locked item refuses any checked replace (the engine returns `ItemLocked`, so every Properties edit is refused), Delete refuses it (`placement::delete_commands`, and Delete and Cut grey while one is selected), and `pcb_geom::tracks_on_pads` leaves a locked track behind when its footprint moves. Routing only adds copper, so it needs no check. The Properties Lock box is live for tracks and vias, the board canvas menu gains Lock and Unlock rows, and `lock_board_items` / `unlock_board_items` take `document=` `ids=` for an agent. The via span is edited by the Via panel's From and To rows and `set_via_span document= ids= layers=A,B`: a replace checks both ends are different copper layers of the board, Top to Bottom is a through via, any other span blind (a micro via stays micro); DRC already reads the span.
