A teardrop is a zone with `fill_type = "teardrop"` (`signex_types::zone_teardrop`), solid copper on the track's net and layer, straight-sided along the track's own line (an off-centre track keeps both sides on it), not tied to its track; adding replaces the nets' old ones; a teardrop that would break the clearance or a keepout is left out and counted. Gloss only merges two selected tracks of one net, layer and width at a free corner into one straight track when it is clear and still touches every copper of its net that touched either track; it reads no router code. Each command takes `document` and its items by uuid on the agent channel and answers with the uuids it added, removed and changed; the seven new board commands join the agent allowlist and are refused inside `apply_edits` (a board step, not a sheet's). Two commands: `import_dxf` (the drawing onto a layer, the active layer of the board in view read when the command runs, or the `layer` operand's token) and `import_dxf_outline` (the largest closed shape by area replaces the board outline), both greyed without a board in view, in File ▸ Import ▸ DXF to Current Layer... and DXF as Board Outline..., the palette and any key. Operands `path` (required), `layer`, `document` (an open board; else the board in view); with operands the file is read off the UI thread without a dialog, the command reports `Deferred` and is answered once the file is read with the summary (`graphics`, `outline_points`, `skipped`, `target`) or the refusal as `Disabled`, the same lines going to Messages. A drawing past the board file's nanometre range is refused (`DxfError::OutOfRange`; `AddGraphics` refuses it too, `BoardCommandError::NotStorable`), so an import never leaves a board that cannot be saved. A file over 64 MiB is not read. Read: `LINE`, `ARC`, `CIRCLE`, `LWPOLYLINE`, `POLYLINE` with bulges; other entities skipped and counted; `$INSUNITS` scales to mm (unitless or missing reads as mm; unsupported units refused). The file origin is the board origin, y up. A layer import appends board drawings through the new `BoardCommand::AddGraphics` (inverse `RemoveGraphics`, refused unless the drawings are still last); an outline import is `SetBoardOutline`, refused while the board sketch owns the outline. One undo step each. A class-to-class matrix cell is a rule of the text, `class == "A" and other_class == "B"` (`NetScope::ClassPair`), so the matrix and the list stay two views of one rule list; it applies to a clearance between a net of each class (either order, both sides), ranks as a class rule with one more narrowing condition (above a one-class rule, below a rule for one net), and an empty cell shows greyed what the pair needs without class-pair rules (`DrcRules::pair_default`, else the larger class clearance with no board open). A named rule set is stored beside the text in `rules.drc.sets` (limits, severity, and each scope it is applied to with the rules it wrote); applying writes one ordinary rule per kind it limits, named `<set>_<scope>_<kind>`, and redefining it rewrites those rules, so Run DRC still reads only the text. Presets: the generic tiers (`FabTemplate` Standard, Advanced, HDI, the minimums the setup dialog already ships, no fabricator named) merge their `fab_*` rules by name; a rule profile holding DRC rules replaces the project's `rules.drc` as "Update from profile" does. Both show the rules added, changed and removed before Apply, and Apply is refused when the rules moved since the preview. Every edit is one undo step on the project's rule history, and an agent reaches each through `rules_matrix_get/set`, `rules_set_create/edit/apply` and `rules_preset_diff/apply` (the design's dotted names written with `_` as the catalog's ids are). | Caner (issue #2398 design) | #2398 | `signex_drc_dsl::table::matrix_tests`, `signex_drc::class_pair_tests`, `app::rules_tab::matrix_tests`, `catalog.golden.tsv`, `builtin_profiles*.golden.tsv`, `commands.json`, `app::agent::allow::tests::the_allowlist_is_the_queries_and_the_document_commands` |

The Route group of the PCB Active Bar gains Differential Pair Routing, Quick Route and Multi-Route as board placement tools (Place rows, `place_board_diff_pair`, `place_board_quick_route`, `place_board_multi_route`); a new Tune group after it holds Length Tuning and Differential Pair Length Tuning (`tune_net_length`, `tune_diff_pair`, palette and agent channel, operands `document`, `net`, `value`; no menu row). A pair is found by name ending (`_P`/`_N`, `_p`/`_n`, `+`/`-`), else by a net class with a pair gap holding exactly two board nets; width and gap come from that class, the gap never under the rule clearance between the nets. Pairs and buses route one centre line around other nets as one wide track (own nets no obstacle), members follow at mitred offsets and are checked one by one; walkaround only, one layer, no end joins yet. Quick Route lands at once and avoids nothing. Pair length tuning lengthens the shorter net to the longer (no coupled meanders yet). Each route and each tuning is one undo step. A bus or pair pitch carries 4 nm more than width plus gap, so corners rounded to whole nm never bring two members under the clearance. The two tune commands are refused inside `apply_edits` (they edit a board, which no sheet's undo step covers). Every tuning surface (the track panel's Tune Net To and the Tune group) takes one accordion shape, `Meander::for_tracks`. | Maintainer (issue #2389) | #2389 | `pcb_geom::route::parallel_tests`, `pcb_geom::route::world::tests::kin_nets_are_no_obstacle_to_each_other`, `signex_engine::pcb::route::{bus_tests, pair_tests}`, `app::handlers::pcb_tool::side_by_side_tests`, `app::handlers::pcb_tune_tests`, `group_tests::the_route_and_tune_groups_list_their_rows`, `menu_bar_place_board.golden.tsv`, `catalog.golden.tsv`, `builtin_profiles*.golden.tsv`, `commands.json`, `app::agent::allow::tests::the_allowlist_is_the_queries_and_the_document_commands`, `agent_edits::wire::tests::queries_history_files_and_project_data_are_refused_with_a_reason` |
| 2026-11-16 | Panelisation (#2410, the panel slice of the issue's design note). The panel is board data, `BoardOptions::panel` (`signex_types::pcb_panel::BoardPanel`: columns and rows, gaps, four rail widths, fiducial and tooling hole diameters, mouse bites or V-score, bite hole, pitch, tab width and tabs per edge), saved as an optional `[board_options.panel]` table in nm and written only when set, so a board without one re-saves byte-identical; this replaces the note's separate panel document naming the source board (a panel on the board cannot go stale, and needs no new document kind; a panel of several boards is left for later). The copies are never stored: `pcb_geom::panel::layout` derives the frame, copies, rails, marks, tabs, bite holes, routed edges and V-scores from the outline's bounding box, and `panel_board` builds the whole panel as one board (each copy's nets suffixed `/n`, fiducials as fixed graphical footprints, holes and scores in the sketch) for the fabrication outputs only. `BoardCommand::SetBoardPanel` is the one way it changes (one undo step, exact inverse, panel rules checked by the engine; that it lays out on the outline is checked by the app before the command, since the outline may change later). Catalog rows `panelize_board` and `remove_board_panel` (Tools menu, palette, Panel section buttons), `set_board_panel` (operands only; the Panel section of the board's Properties is its surface) and the query `board_panel`, all naming their board and on the agent channel, refused inside `apply_edits`. The board view previews the panel behind the board. Behaviour changes: (1) Tools gains Panelize Board and Remove Panel after Re-Annotate Board; (2) the board Properties gain a Panel section after the Guide Manager; (3) fabrication outputs write V-score lines on a new `<name>-vscore.gbr` (`Vcut`) image and the `VScoresNotWritten` note and its message are gone; (4) Fabrication Outputs on a panelised board writes the whole panel unless its new box is unticked. | Caner (#2410) | #2410 | `pcb_panel::tests`, `format::board_options_tests`, `pcb_geom::panel::tests`, `pcb::panel_tests` (signex-engine), `fab::tests::panel`, `app::board_panel::tests`, `pcb_canvas::panel_preview::tests`, `menu_bar.golden.tsv`, `catalog.golden.tsv`, `builtin_profiles*.golden.tsv`, `commands.json`, `docs/COMMANDS.md`, `app::agent::allow::tests::the_allowlist_is_the_queries_and_the_document_commands` |
| 2026-11-16 | Pin and gate swap and design reuse blocks on a board (#2410). Four catalog rows (PCB, no default key, each on the board menu, the palette and the agent channel): `swap_pins` and `swap_gates` (live with two pads of one unlocked component selected; operands `document`, `designator`, `pins=a,b` / `units=a,b`), `save_board_reuse_block` (live with board objects selected; operands `document`, `name`, `ids`) and `place_board_reuse_block` (a board menu row per block of the project, live with one unlocked component selected; operands `document`, `name`, `designator`, `degrees`; without operands it says what it needs). The swap rule is the part's: its `Pin Swap Groups` and `Gate Swap Groups` parameters (`1,2;5,6,7`), and the component's Pin Swapping / Part Swapping switch must be on; a part without the parameter allows no swap. A swap trades pad nets on the board and rewires the linked sheet (a pin swap moves what ends on the two pin tips; a gate swap trades the two units' unit numbers) as one undo step across both, the board and sheet steps sharing one undo group as the #2366 footprint swap does; it is refused with nothing changed when the sheet is closed or read-only, a rewired wire would join nets, or the gates span sheets. Reuse blocks are stored per project as `<project folder>/reuse-blocks/<name>.snxpcb` (written off the update thread; kept in the Model and placeable until Signex closes if the write fails) and read once when a board menu first opens or a placement by operands needs them. A block holds footprints, tracks and vias only; placing finds counterparts through the schematic units the sheet placements share, keeps the anchor's place, and is one board undo step. A block name that differs from a project block's only in case, or is a reserved device name (`CON`, `NUL`, `COM1`, ... before any dot), is refused, so no save overwrites another block's file on a case-blind file system; a save is refused while the project folder is being renamed. A Place row is a context-menu action (`ContextAction::ReuseBlock`): it closes the menu and acts on the board of the window it was in, whose project's blocks it lists. Swap Gates on two pads of one gate is refused. The undo history names the group "Pin or gate swap". Updating placed copies when a block changes is a later slice. | Caner | #2410 | `handlers::pcb_component::part_swap_tests::*`, `board_reuse::tests::*`, `board_reuse::store::tests::*`, `view::context_menu::tests::board_view_rows::the_board_menu_swaps_pins_and_saves_and_places_reuse_blocks`, `signex_engine::pcb::pad_swap::tests`, `signex_engine::pcb::reuse_block::tests`, `signex_engine::part_swap::tests`, `signex_types::part_swap::tests`, `catalog.golden.tsv`, `builtin_profiles*.golden.tsv`, `commands.json` |
| 2026-11-16 | Placement extras on a sheet (#1667, as listed on the issue; `rubber_stamp_copy` and `repeat_last_item` already had handlers). `autoplace_fields` runs new engine command `AutoplaceFields`: the reference and value of the selected parts (a selected field stands for its part) go where the autoplacer puts them, fields the user moved included, and the part's fields count as autoplaced again; one undo step, refused on a locked part (from the key, a locked part in the selection is skipped and named in Messages and the others are placed). `copy_attributes_or_add_vertex` copies the object under the cursor (a context menu's point, else the cursor, else the one selected object): a part arms another of the same part with the next free designator, a net label or port arms the label tool with its text, shape and text style, a text note arms the text tool, and the copy follows the cursor as the tool's ghost; while a wire or a shape is being drawn it says that adding a vertex is not available yet and does nothing. `place_design_block` asks for a `.snxsch` file and `import_graphics` for a DXF file; the file is read off the UI thread and its content lands on the cursor like a paste (Space turns it, X / Y mirror it, one click places it as one undo step, designators numbered as a paste numbers them); a read that lands after Esc, a tool change or a sheet switch is dropped. A design block carries parts (with their bodies), wires, buses, bus entries, labels, junctions, no-connects, text notes and drawings; its sheet symbols are left out and counted in Messages. Import Graphics reads ASCII DXF LINE, CIRCLE, ARC, LWPOLYLINE and POLYLINE, scaled from `$INSUNITS` to mm (none: mm), Y turned down, drawn at the project's shape line width; polyline bulges are read as straight segments, a circle, arc or polyline on a plane facing down Z (extrusion `0,0,-1`) is mirrored back in X, other entities are counted as skipped, more than 50 000 drawings or a file over 64 MiB is refused. Menu rows: Place ▸ Design Block... and Place ▸ Import Graphics... after Sheet Entry, and Part Actions ▸ Autoplace Fields and Copy Attributes on the sheet right-click menu; new menu labels Autoplace Fields, Copy Attributes, Design Block... and Import Graphics.... Catalog changes: `import_graphics` moves from `library` to `place`, needs a sheet and is no longer `gui_only`; `place_design_block` is no longer `gui_only`; both take `path`, `at` and an optional `document`; `autoplace_fields` takes a required `id` and `document` and so joins the agent channel's allowlist. An edit list (`apply_edits`) refuses any command that takes a file path. | 2026-11-17 | DRC waivers: one store, with a geometry fingerprint (#2307, decision https://github.com/alplabai/signex/pull/2455#issuecomment-6955039336). **Maintainer decisions:** (1) DRC waivers live only in the `.snxpcb` board options (`BoardOptions::drc_waivers`), the store the DRC panel's Waive and `waive_drc_violation` / `unwaive_drc_violation` already use (#2399); the `.snxprj` `rules.drc_waivers` list is not added. (2) A board-file waiver gets a geometry fingerprint: a waiver whose objects changed lapses and the finding comes back, listed as lapsed, as in ERC; it covers per-layer pad and via sizes, every `ItemRef` kind, and a newer waiver winning over an older one with the same identity. (3) `check_drc` reads the board-file waivers, the same ones the panel and Run DRC read, and fills `waived` / `lapsed`; its waiver identity is the key `waive_drc_violation` takes (`evidence.waiver_key`). (4) One JSON adapter for a DRC waiver in reports. Review point 5 asked for one adapter shared with ERC: `signex_types::check::finding_waiver_json` writes the shared fields (`kind`, `rule_id`, `fingerprint`, `reason`) and both `signex_erc::check::finding_waiver` (adding `objects`, `author`, `date`, `message`, its JSON unchanged) and `signex_drc::waiver_json` (adding `key`) call it. **Implementation choices:** `DrcWaiver` gains `fingerprint` (written only when set); a waiver without one (made before this change by #2451, which 26.11.0 shipped) never matches, so after the update it lapses once and is waived again; `signex_drc::waiver_state` takes a same-key waiver whose fingerprint matches, else the last same-key waiver lapses; the panel shows a lapsed finding as an open row and a run logs one Messages line when waivers lapsed, except for a run the board was edited during; Waive and `waive_drc_violation` are refused on stale results ("run DRC again before waiving"), unwaiving is not; the writer stores the fingerprint and keeps one waiver per key, so waiving again replaces a lapsed waiver; a footprint is fingerprinted by its placement, pads and copper and courtyard graphics, not silkscreen. Supersedes the 2026-11-13 `check_drc` rows' "`waived` and `lapsed` stay empty" and, for #2399, a waiver holding whatever its objects do. See `docs/architecture/pcb-drc.md`, "Waivers". | Maintainer (numbered decisions, posted by Caner, 2026-11-17); implementation choices pending maintainer ack in #2455 | #2307, #2399 | `signex_drc::waiver::tests`, `signex_drc::findings_tests::{a_board_file_waiver_moves_its_finding_to_waived, a_lapsed_waiver_is_listed_and_its_finding_is_back, a_waiver_of_a_rule_switched_off_neither_hides_nor_lapses}`, `signex_types::format::board_options::tests::{a_waiver_without_a_fingerprint_loads_and_re_saves_unchanged, a_waiver_fingerprint_round_trips}`, `app::handlers::pcb_drc_results_tests::{a_waiver_stores_the_fingerprint_of_its_objects, a_moved_object_lapses_its_waiver_and_a_new_waiver_replaces_it, a_waiver_on_stale_results_is_refused, a_waiver_after_a_net_class_change_is_refused, a_run_edited_during_its_check_logs_no_lapsed_waivers}`, `app::command::queries::check_drc_tests::a_board_file_waiver_is_applied_and_lapses_when_its_track_moves`, `signex_types::check::tests::a_finding_waiver_has_the_shared_fields_and_the_check_s_own`, `signex_erc::check::tests::a_finding_waiver_says_what_it_waives_and_why` |

The source is saved; the canvas, the PDF, SVG and print preview draw the value with its derived unit, and the Properties Value row shows the source with the result or the error under it. Maintainer decision (2026-11-18, review finding 4): the BOM (its lines and its grouping), the netlist export, ERC rules and the Net Inspector use the evaluated value too, so two parts with the same `= Rbase * 2` and different `Rbase` are two BOM lines. A field that does not evaluate (unknown designator, unit mismatch, a cycle across parts, a designator typed with the full-codes `-` sign, which is display-only: `= -QAC1.Value` is an error, not a negation) keeps its source text everywhere and is reported in Messages when its sheet is saved (Ctrl+S, Save All, a save before a close) and when a BOM or netlist export reads it. | 2026-11-18 | Rule-specific No-ERC directives (#1699): a No-ERC directive is its own type, `NoErcDirective`, split out of `NoConnect`, with a list of ERC rule ids it silences; an empty list silences every rule, which is how every marker saved before the list loads. The list is written only when it names rules, so an old sheet re-saves byte-identical. Every suppression path (the per-sheet run, Pin type conflict, Net has multiple names, and the project-wide names and units passes) uses `no_erc_rules_suppress`. Properties edits the list with one checkbox per rule plus All Rules, one undo step each (`Command::SetNoErcRules`); unticking the last listed rule returns the marker to every rule. The engine stores every list sorted without repeats (`*` as empty), so files do not depend on pick order. Automation: `place_no_erc` gains an optional `rules` operand, and a new catalog command `edit_no_erc_rules id [rules] [document]` (no menu row, like `edit_blanket`; reachable through `apply_edits`) sends the same `SetNoErcRules`; `rules` left out or empty means every rule, an unknown rule id is refused. New operand name `rules` (text) in the operand convention. Copy, cut and paste now carry No-ERC directives. A sheet where any marker names rules is written as `snxsch/1.2`, chosen by content as `.snxsym` does, and a sheet without a list stays `snxsch/1.1`; a build that predates the list reads up to 1.1, so it opens a 1.2 sheet read-only and refuses its saves instead of dropping the list (an older `NoConnect` read ignores the unknown `rules` key, so the token is the only guard). | Caner (2026-11-18) | #1699, #2207 | `format::no_erc_tests`, `no_erc_directives::{a_listed_rule_is_suppressed_and_another_rule_at_the_point_still_reported, a_directive_for_another_rule_leaves_the_diagnostic}`, `units_not_placed::the_project_pass_skips_a_no_erc_directive_for_another_rule`, `no_erc_directive::{a_rule_list_edit_is_one_undo_step, properties_rule_checkboxes_edit_the_list_one_undo_step_each}`, `selection_payload::tests::a_no_erc_directive_copies_and_pastes_with_its_rules`, `command::no_erc_operand_tests`, `catalog.golden.tsv`, `scripts/catalog-ids.txt` |
| 2026-11-18 | Bottom-up hierarchy (#1276): two new catalog commands with operand forms (everything is controllable from automation: `create_sheet_symbol_from_sheet path=.. [at=x,y] [allow_merge=..] [document=..]` and `sync_sheet_entries symbol=<id or name> direction=to_symbol|to_sheet [document=..]`, rules in `docs/CLI.md`, "Bottom-up hierarchy"), both new rows of the Design menu after a separator and of the sheet menu's Sheet Actions flyout. Design ▸ Create Sheet Symbol From Sheet... (`create_sheet_symbol_from_sheet`) picks a `.snxsch`, reads it off the UI thread and places one sheet symbol (`Command::PlaceChildSheet`, one undo step) with an auto-generated entry for every port the file exposes, outputs on the right edge and the rest on the left, one grid apart. Design ▸ Synchronize Sheet Entries and Ports... (`sync_sheet_entries`) opens a dialog for the selected sheet symbol that previews the names either direction would add, remove and rename; Apply makes exactly those changes as one undo step, Esc and Cancel change nothing. Sheet to sheet symbol is `ReconcileChildSheetPins`, which gains `renames` for the one sheet symbol the preview was made for, on its auto-generated entries only (a renamed entry keeps its place, id, direction and wires; a hand-placed entry, or an entry of another sheet symbol of the same file, keeps its name); Symbol to sheet renames, deletes and places Hierarchical labels on the child, never a Global label, through the grouped sheet write (a closed child goes to a tabless engine, left unsaved, refused when its file changed since the read). `sync_sheet_pins` and its operand form are unchanged. Implementation choices (not in the decisions): a rename is read only when one direction has exactly one name gone and one new, else they stay a removal and an addition; Sheet to sheet symbol removes auto-generated entries only, as Sync Sheet Pins does; the new symbol is named after the file stem (`stem_2`, ... when taken), refers to the file relative to the sheet, sits at the cursor's last point on the sheet, else under the sheet's content, and is refused for the sheet itself, a file that places the sheet one level down, a sheet above it at any depth (the Navigator's hierarchy in the dialog, `SheetHierarchy::sheets_above`; the operand form runs `place_sheet_symbol`'s full checks, frozen name rule and `allow_merge` included), and a file the sheet already places; the operand form of `create_sheet_symbol_from_sheet` takes a file path, so it is no `apply_edits` edit (as `place_design_block`), and a headless run without `path` goes to the operand handler and is refused for the missing operand instead of opening a file picker it cannot answer; `sync_sheet_entries` is an `apply_edits` edit, but `direction=to_sheet` is refused inside a list or an agent's one-step run because it edits the child sheet; the menus gate both rows on a sheet in view (`Needs::Schematic`), the sync asks for a sheet symbol when none is selected; an added port goes in a column at the sheet's left, on the first row that connects to nothing. | Caner (2026-11-18, batch 5 brief) | #1276 | `signex_engine::sheet_sync_tests`, `app::sheet_sync::tests`, `command::sheet_sync_operand_tests`, `agent_edits::wire::tests`, `menu_bar.golden.tsv`, `catalog.golden.tsv`, `builtin_profiles.golden.tsv` |

Every row name is a token in any text, note or title block (`${CompanyName}`, `${Author}`, `${Address1}`, `${CurrentDate}`, `${CurrentTime}`, `${Time}`, `${ModifiedDate}`, `${DocumentFullPathAndName}`, ...), matched exactly and before the variables, as the upper-case tokens are. The clock is read in the runtime layer at each panel refresh and kept in `DocumentState::times` (view and resolver read state only); the time shows to the minute, so it changes, and redraws the canvas, at most once a minute and only when a refresh happens; it is the time the panel was last built, not a ticking clock. The modified time is taken on the read worker (`DiskRead::modified`) and set to now when a save lands. | 2026-11-18 | A dragged wire keeps its connections (maintainer's hands-on report, 2026-11-18). With "Keep wires connected when moving" on (the default; Alt flips it for one drag), a selected wire carries what joins it, by the rubber-band rules pins already use: its two ends, and every junction dot and child-sheet pin on its span. An unselected wire on such a point stretches, shrinks or bends to stay orthogonal, and folds away when it shrinks to zero length; a pin, label or locked wire there keeps its place and gets a new orthogonal stub to the wire. A dragged wire never makes a connection the plain move would not: where carrying one of its points would turn a dotless T into a dotted one, the whole wire moves plainly; a pin moving with the wire by the same offset rides on it, and a pin moving by another offset keeps its own point. A wire dragged onto another net joins both nets: its old connections are kept and its new end connects (confirmed by Caner, 2026-11-19). A part pin touching a wire mid-span without a dot was never on the net and gets no stub. A selected wire now shows a square grip on each end; dragging a grip moves that end alone (new engine command `MoveWireEnd`, one undo step): a horizontal or vertical wire stays on its line, a wire attached square to that end moves sideways with it and keeps its own joins, other attached wires follow the end, the end leaves a pin it sat on, and the grip snaps to the grid and to pin tips, wire ends and dots. A junction dot is drawn in the colour most of its wires share (their own, net or class colour), on the canvas, in SVG / PDF / print and in the drag ghost; on a tie, or when the default colour leads, the dot keeps its earlier rule (class colour, with a net colour over it). This supersedes the 2026-11-08 row's silence on dots and the rule that a net colour alone leaves a dot at the theme colour, for dots whose wires carry the colour. Rules in `docs/RENDERING_RULES.md` ("sch-junction-colour") and the `transform::rubber_band::wire_contacts` module docs. | Caner (maintainer report 2026-11-18) | — | `wire_drag_tests::*`, `exec::patch_exactness_tests` (`MoveWireEnd` samples), `canvas::wire_grip_tests::*`, `drag_layers::tests::a_dragged_wire_marks_the_joins_on_its_span`, `schematic::junction_color::tests::*`, `schematic_runtime::wire_style_render_tests::{a_junction_on_coloured_wires_is_drawn_in_their_colour, a_junction_follows_the_net_colour_of_its_wires}`, `svg::wire_style::tests::a_junction_prints_in_the_colour_of_its_wires` |
| 2026-11-18 | Board tools draw on the active layer; a route from a pad that does not reach it starts with a via. Interactive Routing, Quick Route and Track started on netted copper that is not on the active copper layer (a surface pad on Top with an inner layer active) take that copper's net and start with a through via there, in the same undo step as the first leg, sized as a mid-route via; a pad that reaches the active layer (through-hole) starts directly; over bare board the start has no net, as before. A leg of Interactive Routing aimed at a pad of another net is refused and the status bar and Messages name the pad and both nets. A route that ends on a pad of no net connects: the pad joins the route's net as a connection made on the board (its net locked), in the route's undo step; the status bar says "U1.1 joined net A" and Update PCB lists every pad whose net is kept that way ("pad U1.1 keeps net A, set on the board"). A leg, Track run or route ending on a pad that does not reach its layer ends with a via there in the same step. Differential Pair Routing and Multi-Route start a net whose copper is not on the active layer with a via, as Interactive Routing does. The leg on the pointer is never dropped silently: when the preview finds no way, the straight leg is drawn in the DRC error colour and the status bar says why. A route or track run on the cursor is drawn at full colour, not dimmed. While a board tool is armed the status bar says how to use it, that it is paused, that it needs a copper layer, or why the last click added nothing. A Solid Region, Polygon Pour or Keepout closes on a click within half a grid step of its first corner, or on a double-click. A copper area takes the net set in Properties, else the net under its first corner on copper; a pour placed with no net, or one with no fill, says why in the status bar and Messages, and a pour's Properties says why it has no fill. | 2026-11-18 | A pin end that lands inside a wire connects to it (maintainer decision 2026-11-18). The edit that makes the contact (placing or moving a part, drawing or moving a wire) adds an automatic junction dot there, in the same undo step, through the engine path that already adds dots where wires meet in a T (`reconcile_wire_junctions`, `transform::pin_junctions`). The dot is drawn and saved like any junction and is checked again on every later edit: it goes when the pin or the wire leaves. An automatic dot never shorts a part: a pin end gets none where its wire, or that wire's net, already holds another pin end of the same part (a wire running along a resistor through both its pins), and such a touch is not reported for repair. That rule applies only when a dot is made: an automatic pin dot stays while its pin end still sits inside a wire, whatever later edits do to the nets. A bare touch that was already there before the edit stays bare, even when the edit reshapes the wire it lies on (a rubber-banded neighbour): only Repair Pin Junctions turns an old touch into a connection. Connections still happen only at dots (#107), so the netlist, ERC, net highlight and the wire drag (stubs for pins on a dragged wire, 2026-11-18 row above) follow from the dot. One exception (2026-11-19 row, gap (a), confirmed by Caner 2026-11-19): with "keep wires" a moved pin held on its own net whose end lands inside a wire of another net gets no dot there and keeps its net, with a notice. This changes the earlier rule that a pin touching a wire mid-span is no connection, and the line of the row above that such a pin gets no stub: a part moved so its pin lands inside a wire of another net now joins that net. A sheet that already holds such a touch with no dot (an older Signex, another tool) is never changed on open: Messages says once per open "N pins touch a wire without a junction; Repair Pin Junctions adds the dots", and the new catalog command `repair_pin_junctions` (Repair Pin Junctions; Schematic group, validation, GUI only) adds them as one undo step. | Caner (maintainer decision 2026-11-18; the no-short, minting-only and old-bare-touch rules confirmed 2026-11-19) | — | `pin_junction_tests::*`, `transform::pin_junctions::tests::*`, `rubber_band_tests::a_pin_landing_on_a_foreign_wire_interior_joins_it_through_a_dot`, `object_lock_tests::a_pin_dragged_onto_a_locked_wire_joins_it_through_a_dot`, `app::pin_junction_repair::tests::*`, `catalog.golden.tsv`, `tests/golden/commands.json`, `docs/COMMANDS.md` |

| 2026-11-18 | `cycle_wiring_mode` is a drag wiring mode with four steps: Auto (the rubber band as before, the default), Horizontal first and Vertical first (an inserted corner leaves the fixed end, or for an anchored pin the old tip, in that direction; the elbow slide is skipped and the other L is the fallback when the first collides), and Straight (no corner, the wire end goes to the pin at any slope). The key cycles it at any time, during a drag or a click-click move too, and the ghost redraws at once. The mode is `signex_types::corner::DragWiringMode`; it lives in `InteractionState` (in-progress interaction state, not a preference and not core-bound) and is kept across drags. `Command::MoveSelection` and `Command::MoveEach` carry it as the plain-data operand `wiring` beside `keep_wires`; only the commit and the ghost of a drag or a click-click move (both commit through `MoveSelected`) pass the current mode, every other move (Move By, align and distribute, placed-object position edits, agent and CLI operands, rotate and mirror) passes Auto, so their wires bend as before. The status bar names the mode while a drag or click-click move keeps wires and the mode is not Auto. `copy_attributes_or_add_vertex` while a wire, bus, line or polyline is drawn fixes a vertex at the context menu's point, else the cursor, by the same path a canvas click there takes, and the run goes on from it (the inverse of `undo_last_segment`); with no cursor on the sheet it adds nothing and says so in Messages; while a rectangle, circle or arc is drawn it says in Messages that it works only for those runs and changes nothing; the draw state is a wire or bus run (`wire_drawing`, `drawing_mode`), or a shape anchor, arc or polyline points while a draw tool (Wire, Bus, Line, Polyline, Rectangle, Circle, Arc) is active; any other tool that holds a first point (Measure, Sheet symbol, Blanket, Compile Mask) still copies attributes, as before. | 2026-11-19 | Gerber and Excellon viewer, reading half (#2539, Community scope). A new UI-free domain crate `signex-gerber` (in the `DOMAIN` set of `scripts/check_arch_guard.sh`; no iced, wgpu, widgets or i18n; no IO, clock or randomness: each reader takes a file name and its bytes) reads Gerber RS-274X with X2 attributes, Excellon drill files and the Gerber job file into objects in exact integer nm in the file's frame, with `pcb_geom::Shape`s, X2 nets, pins and aperture functions. Nothing is dropped silently: every command not understood and every object that cannot be built is a `ReadProblem` with file, line, the line's text and one kind; an object whose shape cannot be built (an undefined macro, a tool without a size) is kept with no shapes for the viewer to mark. Severity is one rule: a missing X2 file function and a missing end command are warnings, a file that is not fabrication data (listed as Not read) is information, everything else an error. Fixture copies of the exporter's golden files must equal their goldens, so a golden refresh forces a fixture refresh. The viewer tab waits for the shared document-tab shell. Rules in `docs/GERBER_VIEWER.md`. | Caner (2026-11-19) | #2539 | `signex-gerber` `tests/exporter_fixtures.rs`, `tests/round_trip.rs`, `tests/read_problems.rs` |
| 2026-11-19 | Gerber Viewer package and Compare with board (#2539, domain half). A package names each layer from its X2 file function, else its job file entry, else its file name (kept as a label, "Fab notes"); copper is L1..Ln by position; legend, mask and paste pair Top with Bottom and copper Lk with L(n+1-k); the default drawing order puts the profile, drill files and drawings on top, then the stack from the top (the job file's list only breaks ties, since it need not be in stack order). Compare with board uses Signex's own exporter as the board side: `signex_output::fab::generate` (Gerber and drill) read back through the same reader, so `FabFrame` (now public) is the one frame rule and no shape or layer rule is decided twice. Objects pair by kind and place within a tolerance per axis (default 0.010 mm); differences are missing copper, extra copper (marked inside a keep-out of its layer; an inner-layer keep-out is checked on every inner layer), drill size mismatch, unmatched layer and not readable; an object the reader could not build is listed as not readable and never as missing copper. A board with no outline leaves every layer unmatched. Read problems go to Messages, one catalog sentence per problem with the file and the line, at the problem's severity. Rules in `docs/GERBER_VIEWER.md`. | Caner (2026-11-20) | #2539 | `signex-gerber` `tests/package.rs`, `tests/compare_with_board.rs`, `package::naming::tests`, `package::order::tests`, `compare::matching::tests`, `compare::objects::tests`; `app::gerber_read::tests` |

| 2026-11-18 | `cycle_wiring_mode` is a drag wiring mode with four steps: Auto (the rubber band as before, the default), Horizontal first and Vertical first (an inserted corner leaves the fixed end, or for an anchored pin the old tip, in that direction; the elbow slide is skipped and the other L is the fallback when the first collides), and Straight (no corner, the wire end goes to the pin at any slope). The key cycles it at any time, during a drag or a click-click move too, and the ghost redraws at once. The mode is `signex_types::corner::DragWiringMode`; it lives in `InteractionState` (in-progress interaction state, not a preference and not core-bound) and is kept across drags. `Command::MoveSelection` and `Command::MoveEach` carry it as the plain-data operand `wiring` beside `keep_wires`; only the commit and the ghost of a drag or a click-click move (both commit through `MoveSelected`) pass the current mode, every other move (Move By, align and distribute, placed-object position edits, agent and CLI operands, rotate and mirror) passes Auto, so their wires bend as before. The status bar names the mode while a drag or click-click move keeps wires and the mode is not Auto. `copy_attributes_or_add_vertex` while a wire, bus, line or polyline is drawn fixes a vertex at the context menu's point, else the cursor, by the same path a canvas click there takes, and the run goes on from it (the inverse of `undo_last_segment`); with no cursor on the sheet it adds nothing and says so in Messages; while a rectangle, circle or arc is drawn it says in Messages that it works only for those runs and changes nothing; the draw state is a wire or bus run (`wire_drawing`, `drawing_mode`), or a shape anchor, arc or polyline points while a draw tool (Wire, Bus, Line, Polyline, Rectangle, Circle, Arc) is active; any other tool that holds a first point (Measure, Sheet symbol, Blanket, Compile Mask) still copies attributes, as before. Outside a draw Copy Attributes is unchanged. | Hakan (2026-11-18) | #1669 | `signex-types` `corner::tests::drag_wiring_modes_cycle_back_to_auto`, `signex-engine` `drag_wiring_tests`, `app::command::bridge::tests::cycle_wiring_mode_resolves_and_cycles_the_drag_wiring_mode`, `canvas::drag_preview::tests::a_changed_wiring_mode_rebuilds_the_ghost_at_the_same_delta`, regression `rubber_band_move::{the_drag_commit_carries_the_cycled_wiring_mode, a_click_click_move_commit_carries_the_wiring_mode, move_by_ignores_the_drag_wiring_mode}`, `canvas::drag_preview::tests::the_next_canvas_event_rebuilds_the_ghost_in_a_changed_mode`, `keyboard_shortcuts_modal::sections::tests::the_sheet_lists_only_shortcuts_that_dispatch`, regression `placement_extras::{add_vertex_while_a_wire_is_drawn_fixes_the_cursor_point_and_the_draw_goes_on, add_vertex_without_a_cursor_adds_nothing, add_vertex_while_a_line_is_drawn_is_a_click_at_the_cursor, add_vertex_while_a_polyline_is_drawn_is_a_click_at_the_cursor, add_vertex_while_a_rectangle_is_drawn_says_so_and_changes_nothing, copy_attributes_mid_measure_or_sheet_placement_still_copies, copy_attributes_with_the_wire_tool_idle_still_copies}` |

| 2026-11-19 | Project tab Requirements look (mockup row 38). Run checks is the section header's primary button; a summary row under it shows the last run's Passed and Failed counts (Not checked when there are any), when it finished, and Add requirement…; the run's notes or why it did not finish go under that row. Each card's header holds the Enabled switch (it replaces the Enabled checkbox), the kind, the kind id, the requirement id read-only in mono, a one-line value summary, the outcome pill and Remove as a trash icon. The id is no longer edited in the tab: `project_set_requirement` with `field=id` still changes it, and `RequirementsMsg::FieldSubmitted` for the id still commits. A passed card is closed to its header and every other card open; a card holding a typed value or a refusal stays open; the expand button flips a card, and the flip follows its entry by requirement id like the typed values (#2547), going with a removed entry. A requirement switched off is dimmed, its fields take no input, and its pill reads Off (before any run too); the counts do not count it as not checked. Fields sit three to a row with the caption above; a refused value keeps its box in the error colours with the reason under that box (a refusal with no box, such as a switch, a remove or an id, shows at the card's bottom); each finding of a failed run is an error box with its measured values. The 2026-11-17 #2339 row's "edit fields in place" still holds for every field except the id. A run's verdict for an entry with no id is read under `req::#<place>` (its 1-based place in the list, the name the check engine gives it), so such an entry gets its Passed or Failed pill and is counted; before, only `req::<id>` was looked up and it always read Not checked. | 2026-11-19 | Command Log panel (mockup row 161). Every catalog command dispatched through `dispatch_command_reporting` (menus, keys, palette, context menus, undocked windows) and every agent socket request whose token matched adds one row, once per outer call; a command run inside another is not logged again. The mock's open choices take the drawn option: the log is kept for the session only (at most 5000 rows, nothing written unless Save log as… is used), Replay runs the recorded arguments (never the current selection) as the person's command and logs a new row, and view commands (category `view` or `select`, changing no document) are recorded but hidden behind More ▸ Show view commands. The CLI line is not the mock's `signex cmd <id> --arg value`, which does not exist: it follows `docs/CLI.md`, `signex-cli run <project file> <command-id> --arg name=value ...` (a point as `x,y`, the text `--arg` takes), and the empty state's key reads `signex-cli commands`. A successful `undo` marks the newest undoable row of its document undone; `redo` clears the last one. | 2026-11-19 | Sketch parameters refuse ambiguous literals (review of #2522, findings 3 and `pi`); for sketch parameters this narrows the 2026-11-18 expression-literals row. A footprint sketch parameter parses in `LiteralContext::Geometry`: a literal whose suffix is only an SI prefix (`10m`, `1u`, `2n`) or an RKM literal (`4k7`) is a parse error there, never a count baked as millimetres, so a parameter either matches the same text typed in a length field or errors. A parameter named after a reserved constant (`pi`) is refused with `ExprError::ReservedName`, because every expression would read the constant instead. One evaluation shares one recursion budget (`MAX_EVAL_DEPTH`) across every reference it follows, so a reference chain of full-size bodies is `ExprError::TooDeep`, not a stack overflow. | Hakan (review of #2522, 2026-11-19) | #1984 | `signex-expr` `si_units::geometry_context_refuses_prefix_only_literals`, `caps::chained_references_with_full_chains_do_not_overflow_the_stack`; `signex-sketch` `parameter::tests::{prefix_only_literals_are_refused_in_a_parameter, a_parameter_named_after_a_reserved_constant_is_refused}`; `signex-bake` `eval_util::tests::ten_m_via_a_parameter_matches_the_field_or_errors` |
| 2026-11-19 | A move with "keep wires" is checked as it is committed (`exec::guarded`). The reference is the plain move exactly as it would be committed (junctions reconciled, wires folded), made once and never edited. The contacts are the moved pins and the points a dragged wire carries; each round carries the wires, follows the pins, reconciles with the pin dots the plain move made (decided on the plain move's nets, never the candidate's), folds, and is checked against the intent: the plain nets with each kept pin on the net at its old tip and each kept carried point joining the net at its old place to the net its wire landed on. A pin or carried point the check blames goes from follow to a forced new path to plain; a carried point that held something and ends plain is named in a notice ("could not keep R7 pin 1 on the dragged wire"), as a wire that cannot be carried whole now is too. A drag of wires alone is checked the same way. Two compositions, confirmed by Caner (2026-11-19), are each the choice that never merges two nets the move did not already join, and each one constant in `transform::rubber_band::intent`: (a) a pin keep-wires holds on its old net whose new tip lands inside a wire of another net joins only its old net, with no pin dot, and a notice names it; (b) a pin left as a plain move ends on exactly the net the plain move gives it, so a carried point may not join that net to another. A round is also stepped down when it leaves a pin end touching a wire it laid with no dot where the plain move leaves none, and when every contact is plain and the nets still differ the plain move itself is committed. With a dragged bus the wire ends past its entries are part of the checked edit, the plain move leaving them where they were. The drag wiring mode (the 2026-11-18 `cycle_wiring_mode` row) seeds every round's Policy, so the followed and carried wires bend as it says; the guard steps contacts down the ladder but never changes the mode, and checks the bent result like any other. A grip drag of a wire end is checked the same way (`exec::wire_end`): the plain end move is the fallback and the grip rule (the wires attached at the old end go with it, a pin or label there is left) the reference; the square neighbours shifted sideways are tried first, then every attached wire following the end, then the plain end move with a notice. | Design from review (2026-11-19); (a) and (b) confirmed by Caner (2026-11-19) | — | `carry_guard_tests::every_contact_plain_commits_the_plain_move`, `carry_guard_tests::the_collected_property_cases_hold`, `rubber_band_tests::random_rubber_band_moves_never_change_the_netlist` (with `carry_guard_tests::check_allowed`) |
| 2026-11-19 | An automatic pin dot never merges two nets (maintainer answer (c), 2026-11-19). Where a pin end an edit puts inside a wire would join it, through the dot, to anything of another net at that point (a second wire passing, overlapping or crossing; a wire end, dot, label or power-port pin there, as in a dotless T), it gets no dot, with "keep wires" on or off and whether the part or the wires moved: the pin is left bare there, both nets stay apart, and Messages names the pin and both nets as the netlist names them ("could not join R1 pin 1 to the wires it ends on: they carry two nets, N$1 and N$2"), one notice per command. Such a touch is not reported as a bare pin touch and Repair Pin Junctions leaves it alone. The notice is data (`signex_engine::EngineNotice::PinDotRefused`), worded by the app through the catalog (`panels-messages-pin-dot-refused-*`), as the other notices this batch added are (`DraggedWireLeft`, `GripEndLeft`, `HeldPinsNotJoined`); the engine's older English warnings stay strings for now. A pin dot's id comes from the host's `IdSource` (`Engine::set_id_source`, #2429). | 2026-11-19 | `snxsch/1.3` for Schematic Refinement content. A sheet holding a harness connector, a harness wire, a harness-typed sheet entry, a union, a Bezier, dimension or table drawing, a Net Parameters directive, or a Blanket (track width, keep-short pour, edge nets) or Differential Pair (class) using a field added with them is written as `snxsch/1.3` (`format::snxsch_token`, chosen by content as for 1.2); a sheet without any stays `snxsch/1.1` or `1.2`, byte for byte. A 1.2 build opens 1.3 read-only and refuses its saves instead of dropping the content. Four new `[extras.sheet]` keys, each omitted when empty: `harness_connectors`, `harness_wires`, `harness_entry_types` (sheet-pin uuid to harness type; an entry whose sheet pin is gone is not written and is dropped on load; a key that is not a uuid refuses the file, naming it) and `unions` (a member naming no object on the sheet is dropped on load, as entries for missing drawings are; a union left empty is kept). Harness objects and unions take part in uuid repair after every older kind. The project settings (channel naming, harness definitions, diff-pair classes) are one optional `.snxprj` key, `schematic_design`, omitted at the defaults; `.snxprj` stays `snxprj/1` because an older build keeps the unknown key and writes it back. | 2026-11-19 | Held keys: a held key runs some commands only once, and its auto-repeat steps them no further, so holding `Ctrl+Space` no longer spins the drag wiring mode and holding `Shift+Space` mid wire no longer spins the corner mode. Key repeat is a keyboard matter, so the rule lives in the input adapter (`app::dispatch::input::key_repeat::runs_on_key_repeat`, next to its only caller), not in the catalog the CLI and agents share; the catalog is unchanged. Run once: every `cycle_*` and `toggle_*` id; `switch_segment_posture`, `next_grid`, `previous_grid`, `view_board_3d`, `flip_board_view`; the selection flips and mirrors `flip_selection`, `flip_selected_x`, `flip_selected_y`, `mirror_x` and `mirror_y` (one undo step per hold); `copy_attributes_or_add_vertex` (a held Insert adds one vertex); and the rotate commands `rotate_clockwise`, `rotate_counterclockwise`, `rotate_selection`, `rotate_selection_cw` while a wire, bus or board track run is open, when they bend the run (`Signex::has_open_run_in`, judged for the key's window: the track run on the board an undocked window shows, else the main window's, by the same `is_for`-and-routing rule the rotate handler's `bend_board_route` reads after the swap, and the wire or bus run, which is app-wide). It holds in every profile; the footprint editor canvas's own `X` is outside the keymap and tracked in #2545. Everything else repeats while held: zoom, undo and redo, nudges and arrows, layer, sheet and tab stepping, find next, and rotate of a selection. The keymap claim swallows a repeat whose stroke, after any pending chord prefix, resolves to a run-once command; that swallow leaves a pending chord and its wait alone, so a held key that is also a chord prefix runs its command once when the wait times out, while a repeat any other consumer swallows still abandons the chord. | Hakan (2026-11-19, the input-adapter design after an independent review the same day) | — (follow-up to #1669) | `app::dispatch::input::key_repeat::tests`, `app::dispatch::input::key_repeat_tests`, `app::handlers::held_rotate_window_tests` |
| 2026-11-19 | The Altium profile on macOS binds `Alt+I` (⌥I) to `copy_attributes_or_add_vertex` next to `Insert`, which a Mac keyboard lacks; on macOS ⌥I is the command's menu hint, Windows and Linux are unchanged. The proposed `Alt+Space` for `cycle_wiring_mode` is not added: ⌥ held during a drag flips whether the wires follow (`canvas/input/keys.rs`, `canvas/move_connections.rs`), so ⌥Space would flip that and cycle the mode at once. Instead the macOS section binds `Shift+Tab` (⇧Tab) to `cycle_wiring_mode` next to `Ctrl+Space`, which macOS takes for the input source once two exist: a held Shift does not change the drag (Shift+click adds to the selection and Shift+wheel pans, `canvas/input/pointer.rs`), and no other consumer owns Shift+Tab. Each override the start wire carries (a width other than 0, a colour) wins over the Tab form's Width and Color for every wire of that route; a field the start wire does not override keeps the Tab form's value or the default, so starting on a default wire changes nothing. | 2026-11-21 | Two wire ends that meet on a pin end get a junction dot (maintainer's hands-on report, 2026-11-21: a wire down onto a GND power port's pin and a wire from that point to the right drew no dot, where wire T-joints did). The point holds three connections, so it is drawn and saved like a T: an automatic dot, minted by the edit that makes the meeting (placing or moving the part or power port, drawing or moving a wire), in that edit's undo step, and checked again on every later edit, so it goes when the pin or one of the wires leaves. A power port's pin counts exactly as a part's pin does. One wire end on a pin is no meeting and gets no dot. The wires and the pin already join there, so the dot changes no net. This widens the 2026-11-18 pin-dot row (a pin end inside a wire) to a pin end where two wire ends meet; the 2026-11-19 rule that a dot never merges two nets is untouched. | Caner (2026-11-21) | — | `corner_dot_tests::*` |
| 2026-11-21 | A pad placed with the Pad tool goes on the active layer (Caner's hands-on report, 2026-11-21: pads placed with Top Layer active came out as grey through-hole pads). With Top Layer or Bottom Layer active the free pad is a round surface pad (`PadType::Smd`) on that copper with that side's solder mask and paste listed, no hole, on the net of that layer's copper under it (`signex_engine::pcb::draw::new_free_pad_on`); the preview and the pad form show the same pad. With any other layer active (a mid-layer, whose surface pad would have no mask or paste, or a layer that is not copper, the Multi-Layer case) it keeps the through-hole default of 2026-11-16. The pad form has no pad type or layer row; making another layer active while Tab pauses the tool changes the pad, and Esc puts the layer back. Dimensions were not changed by this row; the dimension row of 2026-11-21 below puts them on the active layer. | Caner (2026-11-21) | #2373 | `signex_engine::pcb::draw_free_tests::a_free_pad_on_outer_copper_is_a_surface_pad_of_that_side`, `handlers::pcb_tool::free_tests::a_placed_pad_takes_the_active_layer` |
| 2026-11-21 | Directives that attach to a wire by a stem get a junction dot at the attach point (maintainer's hands-on report, 2026-11-21: "net parameters and others should get joint dot too"). The stemmed directives are the Net Parameters flag (always) and a Differential Pair that names a class (its flag stands on a stem; a pair without a class draws only its two strokes and gets no dot); a Net Blanket and a Compile Mask are regions with no stem, and a No-ERC directive is a mark with no stem, so none of those gets one. The dot is an automatic, saved junction like a wire T's, minted by the edit that puts the stem on the wire (placing, editing or moving the directive, or drawing or moving the wire) in that edit's undo step, and dropped once the stem or the wire leaves. Inside a wire it is minted only where that one wire passes and no pin end or other wire end stands; on wire ends only where two wire ends meet, or a wire end and a pin end, so the dot never joins anything the wire did not join already (the 2026-11-19 rule that a dot never merges two nets). Net labels are not stems and still get no dot. | Caner (2026-11-21) | — | `stem_dot_tests::*` |

The Placement Paused banner of the sheet and footprint editors shows over the board the paused tool is armed on, in its own window only. Tab, Enter or the banner's Resume goes on with the edited values; Esc puts back the values, the active layer and the width and net of the run being drawn as they were at Tab, and goes on placing. | 2026-11-21 | Holding Ctrl (Cmd on macOS) while dragging a part on the board moves the part alone (Caner's hands-on report, 2026-11-21): the tracks with an end on its pads stay where they are, so those ends no longer touch the pads, and DRC and the ratsnest show that as for any open end. Without Ctrl the ends follow as before (#2379). **Decided by Caner (2026-11-21):** Ctrl latches for the rest of the drag. The moment Ctrl is down, at the press or later, the part leaves its tracks until the drop or cancel, even after Ctrl is released; releasing it never reattaches them, and the next drag starts unlatched. This replaces the earlier live switch, for footprints and vias (tracks and copper arcs) alike. The drag preview and the status bar ("Dragging with tracks (Ctrl: leave the tracks)" / "Moving without tracks (Ctrl pressed: they stay until the drop)") follow it. The drop is one undo step either way. Ctrl with Shift or Alt on a part still draws a box. | 2026-11-21 | The `targets` operand kind (#2326 slice 6 prep): uuids, comma separated, the items a command acts on. One parser (`command::operands::parse_targets`): empty parts skipped, a repeated uuid counted once in the order first given, an empty list, a part that is not a uuid or more than `MAX_TARGETS` (10 000, which bounds one call's per-item scans) refused before any handler. The operand keeps its name `ids`. It is the kind of `ids` on `lock_board_items`, `unlock_board_items`, `set_via_span`, `set_layer_sizes`, `set_pad_shape`, `gloss_selected_routes`, `board_shape_from_selection`, `keepout_from_selection`, `arrange_in_box` and `measure_board`; `save_board_reuse_block` (designators too) and `read_document` (a filter) keep a text `ids` until #2709 (`IDS_STILL_TEXT`, which only shrinks). An agent may send `ids` as a JSON list of uuid strings; it reads as the comma form. `stitch_vias id=` (one zone) is unchanged: empty comma parts skipped, more than one zone refused. An id naming nothing the command acts on refuses the whole command (`board-target-unknown`), never a silent skip. Behaviour changes: (1) `gloss_selected_routes`, `board_shape_from_selection` and `keepout_from_selection` refuse a list with an id that is no track (no track or zone for a keepout) of the board, where they ran on the rest; (2) an id named twice acts once (a lock answers it once; `measure_board ids=a,a` is refused as one item); (3) `keepout_from_selection` refuses a list naming both zones and lines (`board-keepout-mixed`), where it made keepouts from the zones and skipped the lines; the GUI selection path still does that until #2709. Operand refusals (`OperandError`) stay English until #2709. | Hakan (2026-11-21) | #2326, #2709 | `command::operands::tests::{a_targets_list_keeps_each_uuid_once_in_its_first_place, a_targets_list_that_is_empty_not_uuids_or_too_long_is_refused, the_ids_still_text_list_names_commands_with_a_text_ids}`, `board_tools::targets_tests`, `agent_edits::wire::tests::ids_as_a_list_of_strings_reads_as_comma_text`, `pcb_query::tests::measuring_one_item_named_twice_is_refused`, `pcb_item_edits::tests::a_lock_naming_an_item_twice_acts_on_it_once`, `test_adapter::hostile_tests`, `catalog.golden.tsv` |

A copper arc follows too and keeps its bend, with a limit on its size: the drag is refused, with nothing changed and a message, when a following arc's moved end would land on its fixed end or the arc would grow to more than 3 times its radius. **Implementation notes (from implementation and review, not decisions of Caner's):** an end follows only at the via's exact position (to the nanometre), on a copper layer the via reaches and on its net, so a short stub to a nearby pad keeps its pad end; a locked track stays. The via and its ends move as one undo step, with a component drag's preview, status text, ratsnest and Ctrl latch. A via with nothing attached moves alone. | 2026-11-21 | The board's Snap Options take effect at every grid and zoom, on every path that places a point: a drag, Edit ▸ Move, a paste, a part on the cursor, Measure and every board tool. | 2026-11-21 | Board snap review fixes. The status bar's and Grid popover's Snap is the master switch on a board: off, no grid, guide, axis or object snap on any path, Set Origin, Arrange in Box, guide placement, Duplicate and a group's turn pivot included; the Snap Options keep their values, and with Snap off a second click within 2 px of a run's end finishes it. The catch radius counts on the grid that applies to what is placed (an extra grid when one wins). A dragged footprint does not snap to its own following tracks or arcs. A hidden layer or hidden object kind gives no snap targets, and All Layers means all shown layers, a layer the stack does not list counting as shown; the Multi-Layer row hides vias, through pads and non-plated holes as targets. Free pads are targets of Pad Centers, Vertices and Edges. A grid pick, grid command or any board edit from an undocked window's Properties acts on that window's board (`PcbBoardMsg::acts_on_board`). Behaviour change, for every document kind: undocking the tab in view moves the main window to the nearest docked tab, where it used to stay on the undocked document and its commands kept editing it; with no docked tab left it stays. The route's walkaround searches on the board grid but never coarser than the 1.27 mm default; its ends stay where snap put them. A grid below 1 nm is refused. | 2026-11-21 | An undocked window's panels act on that window's document, for boards, sheets, footprints and symbols. Every panel message runs in the window unless `PanelMsg::runs_in_window` names it app-wide (preferences, session filters, project-wide views, what opens a document); the match has no wildcard, so a new variant must be classified to compile. Component Editor windows hold no document canvas and are not scoped. Undocking a tab that is not in view leaves the main window and its project where they were. Undocking the tab in view moves main to the nearest docked tab and ends what main had under way on it: a route lands, a carry, Measure, a guide or origin pick, Arrange in Box and a wire being drawn end. A wire drawn in another window is left alone, and one drawn in a window ends when that window closes. A re-docked tab becomes the active tab, as before undocking moved main, and main's work on the tab it leaves ends the same way. With no docked tab left main stays on the document, so its commands still edit it: two views of one document, one undo history. Grid Properties and the Custom Selection Filter remember the document they opened on and Apply writes there; if it has closed, nothing is applied and Messages says so. A DRC rule link in a window names the project from that window's board and opens the Rules tab on the main window. While a route with a net is running, footprint origins are not snap targets at all; its pads remain targets through Pad Centers, Vertices and Edges, and a route of no net and the first click keep origins. The Track tool refuses a leg ending on a pad of another net on any copper layer, with Interactive Routing's message, so a click off the pad's layer adds no via that shorts; an end via reads the pad's size on the leg's layer. For Track and Interactive Routing alike, every pad at a leg's end is judged, part pads and netted free pads, whatever the board's order: those on the leg's layer when no via is needed, those on the via's span when one is, and any of another net refuses the leg; a mid-run layer-change via at the anchor is judged the same way. The same holds for what such a panel opens: Grid Properties, the custom selection filter and the grid picker apply to the document they were opened on (Grid Properties from main ignores another window's picker), and board reports and exports read the window's board, or the board of the dialog that is open. | review fixes | reviews of `5763900e0..c169e48e5`, `c169e48e5..17633e186`, `17633e186..820e6a0cc` and `820e6a0cc..d00c5c4dc` | `pcb::route::ends_tests`, `handlers::pcb_snap_options_tests::a_netted_route_ignores_every_origin_and_a_no_net_route_keeps_them`, `pcb_tool::layer_tests::{a_track_run_is_refused_on_a_pad_of_another_net_off_its_layer, a_mid_run_layer_change_via_is_refused_on_a_pad_of_another_net}`, `pcb::route::tests::an_end_via_reads_the_pads_size_on_the_legs_layer`, `panel_scope::{grid_properties_from_main_ignores_another_windows_picker, a_stale_window_drawing_flag_does_not_end_mains_wire, a_symbol_window_is_a_symbol_editor_with_main_on_a_sheet, a_board_report_in_a_window_reads_its_board}`, `panel_scope::{grid_properties_opened_in_a_window_applies_to_its_footprint, a_custom_filter_opened_in_a_window_applies_to_its_footprint, grid_properties_apply_does_nothing_once_its_footprint_is_closed, a_drc_rule_link_in_a_window_opens_the_rules_tab_on_main, a_redocked_tab_ends_the_wire_main_was_drawing, closing_a_window_ends_the_wire_drawn_in_it, undocking_main_tab_keeps_a_wire_of_another_window}`, `dispatch::window_action::tests::panel_scope::{a_footprint_panel_edit_changes_only_the_windows_footprint, a_symbol_panel_edit_changes_only_the_windows_symbol, a_sheet_panel_edit_changes_only_the_windows_sheet, app_wide_panel_messages_are_not_scoped, undocking_another_tab_leaves_main_where_it_was, a_redocked_tab_becomes_the_active_tab, undocking_the_sheet_in_view_ends_a_wire_in_progress}`, `handlers::pcb_one_grid_tests::{an_undocked_panel_edits_only_its_own_board, a_fast_grid_chip_edits_the_extra_grid_in_effect}` |
| 2026-11-21 | On a board tab the board's grid is the one grid. The status bar value, its Grid popover and current-row tick, the Grid Manager, the drawn grid, snap, the snap catch radius, the Fast Grids chips and the grid-step commands (`next_grid`, `previous_grid`, `fast_grid_1`, `fast_grid_2`, `cycle_fast_grid`) all read and write the board's own step, and each shows at once in the others; when an extra grid is in effect the status bar, its popover, the Fast Grids chips and the grid keys read and write that grid instead (see the status bar row). A change is one undo step and marks the board dirty, as the step is document data; picking the step it already has is no edit. A sheet keeps the app-wide grid and a symbol its library's, and the status bar shows the one in view; an undocked board shows its own. **Implementation notes (not decisions of Caner's):** a board pick no longer writes the app-wide grid or its preference. A preset held as `f32` is stored rounded to the nanometre, so 0.1 stays 0.1. A step too fine to draw is drawn at 5^k times it from the board origin (#2351), so every drawn line passes through snap points. A board with no setup takes a grid: it gains a setup with every other value unset (0, which every reader takes as its default), and undo gives back no setup (`BoardCommand::RemoveSetup`). | Caner (2026-11-21) | hands-on report | `handlers::pcb_one_grid_tests`, `pcb::grid_tests::a_board_without_a_setup_takes_a_grid_and_undo_gives_none`, `dispatch::board_status::tests::a_grid_preset_or_custom_pitch_sets_the_grid_and_a_bad_one_is_refused`, `pcb_status_counters::{the_board_grid_popover_sets_the_grid, an_undocked_board_shows_its_own_grid}` (regression), `pcb_canvas::grid::tests::{a_dense_pitch_steps_by_five_to_the_first_level_past_the_floor, a_snapped_point_lies_on_a_drawn_line_of_an_off_grid_origin}` |

| 2026-11-21 | A board sketch shows the board's origin. The origin marker sits at `BoardOptions::origin`, the same board point as on the PCB view, with the PCB view's marker and colour, and follows the board's origin-marker visibility; no sketch origin is drawn at model (0, 0). The status bar cursor, a selected Point's X and Y in Properties and a polar pattern's centre read from the board origin with Y up, as on the PCB view. This is display only: the stored sketch coordinates do not change. The sketch's grid, guides and angles follow the board origin too: the grid lines and grid snap step from the origin, so a grid point reads as a round multiple of the step; guide positions show and take values from the origin with Y up; typed and shown angles run counter-clockwise on screen, and Make parameter keeps the number shown. A footprint sketch keeps its origin at the footprint's (0, 0), and its grid, guides, angles and readouts are unchanged. Implementation notes (not decisions of Caner's): stored guides and sketch coordinates stay in model axes, and every Angle dimension reads and writes through one mirror (`signex_sketch::mirrored_angle::mirror`): shown = mirror(stored), stored = mirror(typed), where a literal negates, `name` and `-name` swap, `-(x)` and `x` swap, and any other `x` becomes `-(x)`; `-(-name)` is `name` in its plain form. Old bound angles are displayed Y-up without changing storage: a bare `slope = 30deg` reads `-slope`, -30, its true on-screen value, and neither its stored text nor its geometry changes on open. A stored form changes only when an edit is committed. Retyping what is shown (normalised text, or the same value to floating-point rounding) changes nothing and takes no undo step, while typing 30 over a stored 30.00004 is an edit. Make parameter puts the shown value or expression in the parameter and stores `-name`, and Replace with value keeps the number shown. Polar pattern sweeps are out of this rule (#2727). A positive typed Arc sweep commits a clockwise-in-model arc, which is counter-clockwise on screen. The sketch's snap chain has no origin target. | Caner (2026-11-21) | hands-on report | `handlers::board_sketch_tests::{the_origin_marker_sits_at_the_boards_origin_and_moves_with_it, the_origin_marker_follows_the_board_views_visibility, a_footprint_sketch_keeps_its_origin_and_readouts, the_status_cursor_reads_from_the_board_origin_with_y_up, properties_read_a_sketch_point_from_the_board_origin, grid_snap_lands_on_the_board_origin_plus_whole_steps, a_footprint_sketch_grid_still_steps_from_zero, a_guide_shows_and_takes_its_position_from_the_board_origin, a_footprint_guide_stores_what_is_typed, a_typed_line_angle_runs_counter_clockwise_on_screen, a_footprint_typed_line_angle_is_unchanged, a_typed_arc_sweep_turns_counter_clockwise_on_screen, a_footprint_typed_arc_sweep_is_unchanged, a_literal_angle_dimension_converts_only_at_the_boundary}`, `handlers::board_sketch::parameter_tests::{a_typed_expression_keeps_the_sense_it_shows, the_same_number_on_a_bound_angle_changes_nothing, an_old_bare_slope_opens_unchanged_and_reads_y_up, a_plain_bound_angle_keeps_its_number_and_takes_a_new_one_y_up, typing_the_rounded_number_cleans_a_stored_angle, make_parameter_on_a_typed_expression_keeps_the_number, inlining_a_parameter_keeps_the_number_shown, a_typed_expression_on_a_footprint_angle_is_stored_as_typed}`, `signex_sketch::mirrored_angle::tests::{mirror_is_its_own_inverse, mirror_takes_each_form_to_its_negation, a_rounded_display_is_not_a_retype}`, `footprint::canvas::draw::background::tests::the_grid_is_laid_from_the_board_origin_on_a_board` |

| 2026-11-22 | Annotate, Force Annotate, Reset All, Reset & Renumber and Reset Duplicates write no sheet file. An unopened sheet loads into a parked, unsaved engine, so a single undo restores every sheet under the one-step Annotate undo, and the file changes only on the user's save. A headless run reports such a sheet as unsaved at its end and needs `save_document` for it. Under the per-sheet setting each sheet keeps its own undo, except Reset Duplicates, which is always one group (implementation default, awaiting confirmation). A reset that cannot read or reach a sheet is refused whole, and one that fails while numbering rolls the others back; Incremental still skips such a sheet and names it. A read-only sheet is left unchanged and named, and refuses Reset & Renumber. | Caner (2026-11-22) | #2760 | `app::handlers::erc::annotate_reset_tests::{one_undo_restores_the_open_and_the_unopened_sheet_and_the_file_never_changes, plain_annotate_leaves_the_file_and_one_undo_restores_both_sheets, reset_duplicates_leaves_the_file_and_one_undo_restores_both_sheets, the_force_annotate_command_leaves_the_sheet_unsaved_for_a_headless_run, a_per_sheet_reset_parks_the_unopened_sheet_with_its_own_undo, a_sheet_that_fails_while_resetting_rolls_the_whole_run_back, reset_all_with_a_sheet_that_cannot_be_read_changes_nothing_and_names_it}` |
| 2026-11-22 | A sheet a project-wide run parks is an open document. Its save and the idle poll compare with the bytes the run read and show the changed-on-disk card; an earlier recovery copy is checked and offered before autosave may write. **Undo until you save:** one undo restores every sheet until the user saves. A clean save releases a tabless sheet's engine; after that, a group undo or redo with a member that is not loaded or no longer holds the step (for every group kind, board groups included) is refused whole before any sheet moves, with one Messages line naming the sheets. This replaces the partial undo that stepped the other sheets. A sheet with an open tab keeps its undo through a save. If only the open sheet was saved, the undo restores both. Reset Duplicates is refused whole for an unreadable or busy sheet. The rule covers all ten multi-sheet undo groups. A refused step stays on top, so undo stops there and older edits on those sheets stay out of reach until the step leaves history. | Caner (2026-11-22) | #2760 | `app::handlers::erc::annotate_parked_tests`, `regression::annotate_undo_scope::{undo_is_refused_whole_after_the_other_sheet_was_closed_and_reopened, undo_is_refused_whole_after_the_other_sheet_history_was_trimmed, undo_is_refused_whole_naming_a_sheet_that_is_not_loaded, redo_is_refused_whole_naming_a_sheet_closed_after_the_undo}` |

| 2026-11-22 | (Which commands replay is now the safe list of the 2026-11-22 "refuses unless proven safe" row.) Command Log Replay acts on what the command acted on (mockup row 161). This refines "Replay runs the recorded arguments" of the 2026-11-19 and 2026-11-20 Command Log rows. A command that acts on the selection records its targets (stable ids, with the document) as the row's arguments. Replay runs it on exactly those items as the person's command, as one undo step and a new row, and puts the current selection back. If a target is gone or its document is not open, Replay is refused, names what is missing and changes nothing; it never runs on the survivors. A command with no target runs again as it is. **Implementation note:** commands that go on using the selection after dispatch (Move, Duplicate, Properties, a dialog on the selection) and rows with no recorded targets (a footprint or symbol editor selection) are refused, because the selection could not be put back or was not recorded. | Caner (2026-11-22) | #2786 | `handlers::command_log::tests::{replay_acts_on_the_targets_and_puts_the_selection_back_as_one_undo_step, a_target_that_is_gone_refuses_the_whole_replay, a_closed_document_refuses_the_replay, a_command_with_no_target_is_refused}`, `tests/regression/command_log.rs::replay_of_an_unlisted_command_is_a_new_refused_gui_row` |

| 2026-11-22 | (Which commands replay is now the safe list of the 2026-11-22 "refuses unless proven safe" row.) Command Log Replay extends to all commands. This supersedes the implementation note of the 2026-11-22 #2786 row, which refused commands that go on after dispatch. A tool or dialog records its finished input (targets plus offset, angle or values) on the row that started it, as one row; started and cancelled, it has nothing to replay. Properties and union edits record their targets and values. Footprint and symbol editor commands record the editor document and selection and replay there. All replay as one undo step, put the person's selection and tool back, and are refused whole, naming what is gone, when a target or document is missing. Replay onto a sheet behind the one in view is refused while a tool is in progress, so it never ends a placement. Nothing repeatable, so refused: a cancelled tool, a step of a tool in progress, a pick at the pointer, Find Similar and Save as Reuse Block. **Not built yet, so still refused or unrecorded:** a dialog or tool that made new objects from its own values (Place Reuse Block, Via Stitching, Teardrops, Keepout and Board Shape from Selection when they add items); an editor command on a sketch or silk selection; editor Properties panel edits other than pin number and name; footprint editor pad drags; multi-document grouped board edits. | Caner (2026-11-22) | #2786 | `handlers::command_log::tests::{a_move_replays_its_offset_on_its_item_while_another_is_selected, a_finished_move_tool_is_one_row_with_its_input, a_cancelled_move_tool_has_nothing_to_replay, a_duplicate_replays_on_its_target_and_keeps_the_clipboard, a_properties_edit_is_one_row_per_field_run_and_a_symbol_flag_replay_is_refused, a_union_replay_is_refused, replay_onto_a_sheet_behind_a_running_tool_is_refused, a_symbol_editor_delete_replay_is_refused, a_symbol_editor_delete_replayed_over_its_own_result_is_refused, a_board_move_replays_its_offset_while_another_part_is_selected}` |
